Jean notes the CS-degree advice may not hold word for word in 2025 with ChatGPT replacing engineers; the mindset of positioning yourself for your goals is what transfers. _[ChatGPT]_ — [2025-04-21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=e9C6UDbAIdw)
- **Life motto 'just say yes': saying yes to things you don't feel like doing is what creates the serendipitous moments that change your life.** (Leah Wyar) The advice came from a boss after a breakup at 30. Leah met her husband at a Halloween party she almost skipped, and got her first job by tossing a resume she had 'no business' submitting into a folder. Saying no too much means you're not going anywhere or meeting anyone. — [2025-04-28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yObnjfFejUI)
- **Once you have kids, a paycheck is not enough: you must feel respected, keep learning and see a future path, and when that stops you have to go.** (Leah Wyar) If she's leaving her kids for 9 hours a day she has to feel fulfilled; she could have toughed out Cosmo another year or two with her thick skin but would not have been happy. — [2025-04-28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yObnjfFejUI)
- **Change is external (like changing jobs) while transition is an internal process with three stages: ending, neutral zone and new beginning.** From William Bridges' book 'Transitions'; Cherie says naming the stages matters because go-go-go people like her rush to the new beginning as a way to avoid processing hurt. _[William Bridges, Transitions]_ — [2025-05-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xPAIT2MpsuY)
- **The neutral zone feels slow and unproductive day to day, but journaling, gym and reflection compound over time and are what make a genuine new beginning possible.** At 35 Jean left her head of product role at Snap after seven years and ended an eight-year relationship and engagement, losing her whole conception of her future; only by slowly working through the neutral zone did she arrive at her current new beginning of moving in with Cherie and starting a venture together. _[Snap]_ — [2025-05-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xPAIT2MpsuY)
- **Steve Jobs' ouster from Apple, years in the creative wilderness, and triumphant return illustrate that the neutral zone can be the source of fresh ideas.** The hosts map his story onto ending, neutral zone and new beginning. _[Steve Jobs, Apple]_ — [2025-05-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xPAIT2MpsuY)
- **Transitions exercise: write down one area of life that is ending or has just ended, how you feel about it, and one possibility that could bloom if you fully embrace the neutral zone.** The framework applies to career, romance, friendship and anything else. _[William Bridges]_ — [2025-05-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xPAIT2MpsuY)
- **Designing Your Life applies design thinking to life through four parts: curiosity, prototyping experiences, reframing problems and radical collaboration, favoring small experiments over endless pondering.** From the Stanford class and book by Bill Burnett and Dave Evans; it cuts wasted time by letting you test ideas quickly. _[Bill Burnett, Dave Evans, Designing Your Life, Stanford]_ — [2025-05-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xPAIT2MpsuY)
- **Prototype a career hypothesis by inventing a low-stakes internship for yourself instead of theorizing about it.** After leaving Snap, Jean took an unpaid one-day-a-week stylist internship at the Albright Fashion Library in LA to test a 20-year-old question of whether she wanted to work in fashion or just enjoy it as a consumer; 20 percent of the job was rearranging a shoe closet, and it was one of the most fulfilling things she has done. _[Albright Fashion Library, Snap]_ — [2025-05-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xPAIT2MpsuY)
- **Design thinking breaks paralysis: when stuck, run a small test, because with a growth mindset you learn something whether or not it succeeds.** The hosts tie the frameworks together: reflect in the neutral zone, then test a new beginning with a mini experiment that gathers information from the external world. — [2025-05-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xPAIT2MpsuY)
- **Life design exercise: pick an area you want to pivot and brainstorm two tiny experiments to test it, sharing them publicly for accountability.** Experiments don't need to be massive; the hosts promise to read and respond to comments. — [2025-05-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xPAIT2MpsuY)
- **Do the thing now: the two most dangerous words are 'not now' because they become 'not ever'.** (Kofi Ampadu) A best friend sent Kofi a VC fund plan he had written in 2012 that overlaps about 85 percent with what he does today at TxO; he took roundabout paths through consulting looking for the perfect time. He also says you are rewarded for visibly trying and may get a shot sooner. — [2025-06-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SYjFmv6vZtE)
- **The real retirement goal is finding work you love enough to keep doing it at 70.** (Kofi Ampadu) Kofi's father, a former cab driver who opened a mom-and-pop shop, is nearly 70 and still working; when he described his typical day Kofi realized 'he's retired' because he loves it. Cherie cites Stanford professors teaching into their late 70s as the same role model. — [2025-06-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SYjFmv6vZtE)
- **Track whether you 'won the day' on three metrics (physical health, mental health, three daily tasks) rather than judging a year by New Year's resolutions.** (Kofi Ampadu) Started in 2020 after 2019 felt like a failure; Kofi logs daily in a spreadsheet with notes on bad weeks, posted weekly scores publicly on Twitter ('Week 1: won 6 of 7'), and reviews year over year to keep going up and to the right. Consultant motto: what doesn't get measured doesn't get tracked, and if you can't pinpoint why a week was bad you can't change it. _[Twitter/X]_ — [2025-06-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SYjFmv6vZtE)
- **Before taking any opportunity, ask what the incentives are, who is involved and what they are optimizing for, and whether that aligns with your values.** The mini exercise for the 'dark side of VC' section, generalizing the lesson that investors optimize for their own bottom line. — [2025-07-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bu50AgK6UIw)
- **Take a portfolio approach in life: like VCs who never put the whole fund in one company, don't put all your eggs in one basket, including in dating.** The hosts joke that this is why people keep a dating 'roster'. — [2025-07-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bu50AgK6UIw)
- **Write down one bet you have honestly been too scared to make.** Final mini exercise, since venture capital shapes the companies we work for, the products we use and the headlines we read. — [2025-07-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bu50AgK6UIw)
- **Huge reinvention goals are paralyzing, so break them into tiny habits that are obvious, attractive, easy and satisfying.** The hosts cite James Clear's four laws from Atomic Habits and the statistic that 90% of people who start a new habit quit by day 10; the goal is to be in the 10% who stick. _[James Clear, Atomic Habits]_ — [2025-07-21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=McWujpZMzLw)
- **Starting over doesn't have to be monumental; treat the start of every month as a checkpoint rather than waiting for New Year's resolutions.** The hosts observe that everyone joins the gym in January and falls off, so giving yourself many opportunities to start anew is healthier. — [2025-07-21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=McWujpZMzLw)
- **Reinventing yourself is a gift and can be fun; humans are change-averse, but forced change can lead to beautiful things if taken in stride with the right frameworks.** Jean suggests the mindset shift of relabeling 'scared' or 'nervous' as 'excited'. — [2025-07-21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=McWujpZMzLw)
- **Celebrities with staying power, like Miley Cyrus, Lady Gaga and Taylor Swift, are the ones who constantly reinvent themselves.** Cherie uses this as evidence that reinvention should be part of everyone's life and career longevity. _[Miley Cyrus, Lady Gaga, Taylor Swift, Leonardo DiCaprio]_ — [2025-07-21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=McWujpZMzLw)
- **Rewrite the narrative of a painful decision: ending an 8-year engagement at 35 is not 'I'm someone who breaks engagements' but 'I'm brave enough to leave something perfect on paper in search of what's better for me.'** Jean says this out loud for the first time on the episode, noting the decision would have been incomprehensible to her a year and a half earlier because she was a different person. — [2025-07-21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=McWujpZMzLw)
- **Write down the old story that no longer serves you, then rewrite it to reflect your growth and future aspirations, and share it with a friend.** This is the official exercise for the Esther Perel section; combined with Atomic Habits and Brene Brown's vulnerability, it forms the sisters' toolkit for starting over at any age. _[Esther Perel]_ — [2025-07-21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=McWujpZMzLw)
- **Step two, 'Let Me': after letting go, proactively assign the freed mental and emotional space to something positive, because the brain hates a vacuum and will otherwise fill it with doom-scrolling.** Jean did this by accident after ending her 8-year relationship and engagement, throwing herself into building Tiger Sisters with Cherie, which friends say 'saved' her. _[Tiger Sisters]_ — [2025-08-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=H-xnPI_rUqk)
- **Settling rarely feels like misery; it usually feels 'fine', which is what makes it so insidious.** It looks like a job that's going fine, a partner who is perfect on paper, or shrinking yourself to keep the peace. They cite an HBR figure that 85% of people worldwide feel actively disengaged at work, going through the motions and calling it a career. _[Harvard Business Review]_ — [2025-08-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUN5LWuRwu0)
- **Test whether you're settling by asking: if I continue on this exact path for 12 months, would I be happy with where I end up?** If the answer is no, or you feel yourself shrinking from the answer, you're settling; choosing comfort now quietly kills off your future self. — [2025-08-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUN5LWuRwu0)
- **Rate your alignment 1-10 in career, relationships and personal identity every quarter; anything under 7 is a beige-to-red flag to re-evaluate.** Cherie does this roughly quarterly; it can be a five-minute check asking 'am I happy here?', accepting that no is a valid answer, then asking why and whether it's in your control or seasonal. — [2025-08-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUN5LWuRwu0)
- **Know it's time to move on by asking: does this version of me exist only for others' comfort, am I becoming someone I admire, and would the woman I want to be say yes to this?** If the answer is no, you have your answer; the questions are scary but knowing is only for the better. — [2025-08-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUN5LWuRwu0)
- **Your zone of excellence can quietly trap you; fulfillment comes from your zone of genius even if it means throwing away what you've built.** Being great at something shiny that others admire makes it easy to settle. Cherie quotes 'sometimes power means letting go of the thing you've built to build something even bigger', which is how she frames leaving a 15-year corporate career to build Tiger Sisters. _[zone of genius, zone of excellence]_ — [2025-08-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUN5LWuRwu0)
- **Write a letter from your five-years-future self describing what she had to let go of to become who she is.** Both hosts have done guided future-self meditations; Cherie burst into tears because future-her was calm, knowledgeable and confident. Stepping into your future greatness reveals what's possible beyond your comfort zone. — [2025-08-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUN5LWuRwu0)
- **Stop settling with micro moves rather than blowing up your life: book an exploratory call, ask the uncomfortable questions, or spend an hour acting like the version of you who knows what she wants.** Doing nothing after recognizing you're settling is the worst option; five or ten minutes of action creates momentum. — [2025-08-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUN5LWuRwu0)
- **Life gets harder before it gets easier after a big change, but the scarier outcome is regret in one, five or ten years.** Breakups and job changes suck for a while and then ease; ask whether you'd be happy still being in the same place in ten years, and take the first step today. — [2025-08-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUN5LWuRwu0)
- **Make big decisions with a four-step decision tree: list options, sketch outcomes, assign probabilities and payoffs, and multiply to get expected value.** The framework forces clarity when stakes are high and combines qualitative and quantitative thinking; it is how to make important life decisions without losing your mind. _[expected value, decision tree]_ — [2025-08-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IyFtCATnRpA)
- **Get a decision out of your head and onto paper; the model does not have to be perfect to beat the swirl.** Mini exercise: pick a decision living rent-free in your brain, map two to three outcomes with values and probabilities, multiply, and compare expected values. — [2025-08-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IyFtCATnRpA)
- **Run your life like a product roadmap using OKRs, with an objective and numeric key results that measure it.** OKRs were invented by Andy Grove at Intel, popularized by Google and Stanford's design program, and used at nearly every tech company; a personal example is objective 'get in better shape' with key results like gym three days a week for five weeks or hit 20% body fat. _[OKRs, Andy Grove, Intel, Google]_ — [2025-08-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IyFtCATnRpA)
- **The most valuable part of OKRs is the exercise of figuring out which key results actually ladder up to the objective.** Breaking a scary goal into actionable milestones makes it less intimidating and forces you to think about what you must really do to achieve the outcome. _[OKRs]_ — [2025-08-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IyFtCATnRpA)
- **Together the three frameworks answer three questions: are you solving the right problem, how to stop spiraling and decide, and what to prioritize.** Jobs to be Done checks the problem, decision trees end the spiral, and OKRs prioritize what matters; the sisters urge listeners to actually do the exercises rather than nod along. _[Jobs to be Done, decision tree, OKRs]_ — [2025-08-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IyFtCATnRpA)
- **Success comes from doing less, but better; your calendar must reflect your priorities or someone else controls your time.** From Greg McKeown's 'Essentialism' (Stanford lecturer who trains execs at Apple and Google). The sisters send calendar invites for everything; if the gym isn't blocked on your calendar, you don't really value it. _[Greg McKeown, Essentialism]_ — [2025-09-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HjebjpDU8-Q)
- **Audit last week's calendar by color-coding green (aligned with values) and red (not), then cut or delegate the red.** Saying yes to every panel, dinner and meeting lets others subconsciously fill your calendar with obligations you feel you must honor. — [2025-09-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HjebjpDU8-Q)
- **Productivity comes first from finding monetizable work you love and a problem that breaks your heart, so you have no desire to procrastinate.** Cherie says a passion you cannot monetize is much harder to sustain; she found hers through a lot of experimentation and now feels procrastination delays her personal mission. She name-checks Atomic Habits and Cal Newport as frameworks but calls her answer more holistic. _[Atomic Habits, Cal Newport]_ — [2025-09-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TkXvlgPXitc)
- **If you do choose to date for money, use that window to invest in your skills, relationships and a business of your own so you no longer have to.** Jean's advice for listeners who cannot be talked out of it: build yourself up so you have a sense of self and are never stuck in a situation you cannot leave. — [2025-10-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=q49Hzzez6Fo)
- **The secret of the rich is to slow down and be extremely clear about what you are doing, what you are solving for, and what motivates you.** (Maggie Sellers) Life got easier for her once she understood why she wanted things, what her role and responsibility was, and what made her happy; that intentionality is the unlock to the next level. — [2025-10-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=u6hxzkt6MOE)
- **As a public figure, keep major life events private (not secret) until you are ready, so you protect your energy and can share from a place of joy.** (Maggie Sellers) Maggie kept her wedding offline given her history of a called-off engagement and public doubt; creators fail and create in public, so learning where boundaries and timing sit is part of building in public. — [2025-10-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=u6hxzkt6MOE)
- **The shine is never the whole story, so dig one layer deeper before choosing a school, job or big decision, and define success on your own terms.** Power move for the week: name one area where you are chasing a brand (school, company, job), ask whether you are chasing it for the right reasons, what experience you will actually get, and whether it is worth it. — [2025-10-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qkNnsxjdnlA)
- **Purpose comes from synthesizing three data sets: your values, your aptitudes and your economically viable interests.** (Suzy Welch) This is the core of the Becoming You class at NYU Stern, which had an 800-student waitlist and is now taught in mega sections; the Values Bridge tool rank-orders 16 values and shows how much you are living each one. _[Becoming You, Values Bridge, NYU Stern]_ — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **The 'authenticity gap' is the distance between how much you hold a value and how much you are actually living it, and it is the source of the 'off' feeling in many lives.** (Suzy Welch) Welch describes students with voice or belovedness as their number one value but a 98-99% authenticity gap, meaning they want it and cannot live it. _[Values Bridge]_ — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **Eudaimonia (self-care, recreation, pleasure, sex) typically conflicts with affluence and achievement, and people who rank all of them high must consciously work out the trade-off.** (Suzy Welch) Welch has eudaimonia at 15 because her joy is in her work; Jean notes her banking background trained her to set self-care aside for achievement, while Cherie's Gen Z-adjacent cohort prizes work-life balance. — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **Knowing your values is not enough; you also have to know your aptitudes, both cognitive and personality strengths, because you can be bad at a job purely because of your personality.** (Suzy Welch) Her example: you could want to be Celine Dion and have a very bad voice. _[Celine Dion]_ — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **The Four Horsemen of dream destruction that stop you living your own life are expectations, expedience, events and economic security.** (Suzy Welch) Expectations from family, taking the easy job that recruits you, events like layoffs or babies that push you off course, and money decisions even when money does not matter to you. She offers a free quiz to identify which horseman is at work. _[Four Horsemen of dream destruction]_ — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **People habitually live B-plus lives for decades and usually need a crisis such as a firing, divorce or breakup to start the self-examination; do the work before the crisis.** (Suzy Welch) Jean says her life looked A-plus from outside (beautiful couple, high-powered job, LA) and it took her engagement ending to see it was not her true self; Welch says otherwise she would have been 45 crying in her car asking 'what have I done?' — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **There is no universal work-life balance; the question is what is your balance, and you should give yourself permission to live it without judging others.** (Suzy Welch) She cites Jensen Huang saying 'I love my work life balance, I work all the time' and says the rank-ordered values let people admit they want fame, money and achievement over eudaimonia. Affluence and eudaimonia rarely track together without inherited wealth. _[Jensen Huang, NVIDIA]_ — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **Reinvention is mandatory because the economy, the world and you all change, and getting fired is a career death you can be reborn from as long as you tell the truth about it.** (Suzy Welch) Fired from Harvard Business Review in her 40s and reported in the Wall Street Journal, she refused a press release saying she resigned; two weeks later Oprah's organization called. She later earned a PhD in her 60s and says you can 'die 1000 times in your career.' _[Harvard Business Review, Wall Street Journal, Oprah]_ — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **Use the 'whole body yes' test: when head, heart and gut align you feel a lifting line of energy; anything that does not feel that way deserves a pause and the question 'do I really want this?'** (Jane Marie Chen) On her book tour she was offered three events in one day in New York, felt contraction, and chose to do just one; for overachievers, the achievement is to not overachieve. — [2025-12-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wL4BAe4lBAM)
- **Success without fulfillment is the ultimate failure.** (Jane Marie Chen) Quoting Tony Robbins: many outwardly successful people are empty inside, always comparing to the next person as the goalposts move, so happiness stays an illusion. _[Tony Robbins]_ — [2025-12-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wL4BAe4lBAM)
- **The three Cs work together: embrace change, have the confidence to act on it, and the clarity to execute; without listening you won't see change coming or have confidence.** (Jim Keyes) The combination eliminates fear and lets you accomplish anything; any one alone is important but the package is the point. _[Three Cs framework]_ — [2025-12-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wondntTp_cQ)
- **Education is freedom: money is only an enabler, while knowledge can't be taken from you and gives freedom of time, choice and the ability to experience the world.** (Jim Keyes) His father, who never passed sixth grade, refused Jim's offer to work to pay for plumbing, saying material things can be taken but not knowledge; his dream was to experience other cultures, which his factory job never allowed. The hosts link it to their catchphrase 'information is power'. _[Education Is Freedom (book)]_ — [2025-12-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wondntTp_cQ)
- **Older leaders stay ahead of frontier sectors by engaging their inner child: never losing wide-eyed curiosity and using creativity to defy the conservatism that creeps in with age.** (Jim Keyes) At 70 he invests in space, advanced energy, AI and AR; he paints, sculpts and writes music without expecting to be good. Learning and fun happen outside the comfort zone, and there's no such thing as failure when trying something new. — [2025-12-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wondntTp_cQ)
- **A physical manifestation of digital work carries outsized meaning, and representation matters: two Asian women on a business-podcast billboard felt like breaking new ground.** Cherie could not name any peers in tech or business who had been on a billboard, and the sisters framed it as a milestone symbolizing the year's growth in a world where everything they make is digital. — [2025-12-15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M4HYavwtWzY)
- **Do a year-end reflection listing your top five to eight wins and lessons, since a win and a lesson often come together.** The hosts model this by framing each 2025 highlight (billboard, rebrand, first sponsor) as both a win and a lesson, and invite listeners to share their number one win or takeaway. — [2025-12-15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M4HYavwtWzY)
- **You don't need motivation, you need a system; you're not behind, you're in transition.** The hosts frame starting over as emotional, habitual, identity and relationship work rather than a single decision, and say it doesn't require January 1st. — [2025-12-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NmdHxviASBg)
- **Change is external but transition is internal, moving through ending, neutral zone and new beginning, and most people rush the new beginning without processing the ending.** Jean uses William Bridges' framework on ending her engagement at 35 after 8 years while also leaving Snap after 7 years; the slow neutral zone of journaling and gym compounds into a new beginning. Steve Jobs' ousting from Apple, wilderness years and return are the other example. _[William Bridges, Transitions, Steve Jobs, Apple, Snap]_ — [2025-12-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NmdHxviASBg)
- **Designing Your Life means small, fast experiments over endless pondering: curiosity, prototyping experiences, reframing problems and radical collaboration.** After leaving Snap, Jean invented a one-day-a-week unpaid stylist internship at the Albright Fashion Library in LA to test a 20-year hypothesis about wanting to work in fashion, even though 20% of it was rearranging a shoe closet. Exercise: pick a pivot and brainstorm two tiny experiments. _[Bill Burnett, Dave Evans, Designing Your Life, Albright Fashion Library, Snap]_ — [2025-12-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NmdHxviASBg)
- **Prototyping breaks paralysis because with a growth mindset you learn something whether the experiment succeeds or fails.** The hosts tie the frameworks together: reflect in the neutral zone, then test a new beginning with a mini experiment to gather information from the external world. — [2025-12-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NmdHxviASBg)
- **Atomic Habits beats the 90% who quit a new habit by day 10: make it obvious, attractive, easy and satisfying, and tie the habit to your identity.** Ask 'what would a healthy person do?'; Duke research says 45% of daily actions are habitual. Exercise: pick one goal, break it into tiny habits, and apply the four laws. _[James Clear, Atomic Habits, Duke University]_ — [2025-12-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NmdHxviASBg)
- **Starting over doesn't have to be monumental; make the start of every month a checkpoint rather than waiting for New Year's.** Cherie notes everyone hits the gym in January and falls off; giving yourself many opportunities to start anew is a beautiful thing, and reinventing yourself is a gift and genuinely fun. — [2025-12-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NmdHxviASBg)
- **Celebrities with staying power are the ones who constantly reinvent themselves.** Cherie cites Lady Gaga, Taylor Swift, Miley Cyrus, Christian Bale and Leonardo DiCaprio's varied roles; Jean jokes DiCaprio could reinvent by dating someone older. _[Lady Gaga, Taylor Swift, Miley Cyrus, Leonardo DiCaprio, Christian Bale]_ — [2025-12-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NmdHxviASBg)
- **Don't do all six frameworks at once; pick one, or follow a 7-day plan.** Day 1 transition, day 2 growth mindset, day 3 prototype, day 4 habits, day 5 vulnerability, day 6 rewrite your story, day 7 reflect. — [2025-12-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NmdHxviASBg)
- **'Just say yes' as a life motto: saying yes creates the serendipitous moments that change your life, and saying no too often means you go nowhere.** (Leah Wyar) Leah met her husband at a Halloween party she almost skipped, and got her first job by putting a resume in a folder she had no business submitting. When her boss asked her to run the Entertainment group (People, EW, People en Espanol) after the Meredith acquisition, she first said no, slept on it, then said yes; it became the best decision of her career. _[Dotdash Meredith, People, Entertainment Weekly, Byrdie, Brides]_ — [2025-12-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KTlJZKNQRDc)
- **Once you have kids, a paycheck is not enough; you must feel respected, keep learning and see a path, and when the respect stops you have to go.** (Leah Wyar) Leaving her children for nine hours a day made fulfillment non-negotiable; she could have toughed out Cosmo another year or two with her thick skin but would not have been happy. — [2025-12-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KTlJZKNQRDc)

### AI & technology (20)

- **Policy stories like the TikTok ban should be understood through the real people whose livelihoods depend on the platform.** Cherie, on TikTok since 2020, points to a Forbes article profiling a creator whose entire income is on TikTok; some creators diversify across TikTok, Instagram and YouTube while others are fully dependent. Practical tip: update the app before the ban since delisting stops future updates. _[TikTok, Forbes, Apple App Store, Google Play]_ — [2025-01-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_8AlNMC2l4)
- **Gen Z holds two competing tenets, privacy-consciousness and anti-establishment sentiment, and the RedNote migration shows the second winning.** Jean, who spent 10 years building products for Gen Z, notes users protesting the US government by moving to Xiaohongshu ('Little Red Book', a Mao reference), an app even more directly tied to the Chinese state than ByteDance-owned TikTok, without most knowing or caring about national security. _[RedNote, Xiaohongshu, ByteDance, TikTok, Duolingo]_ — [2025-01-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_8AlNMC2l4)
- **Meeting notes will become a commodity; AI is moving from assistant to agent to optimization of outcomes across all channels.** (David Shim) Like ad tech unifying exposures and conversions, combining meetings, messages, calls and emails lets AI say '12 clients complained about this feature, want me to draft a PRD?'; Read AI's roadmap includes a Chrome extension summarizing email and auto-complete that fills answers from your meetings. _[Read AI]_ — [2025-01-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tWxcgyd051c)
- **The opportunity is now: AI is today's sea change like mobile was, and incumbents like Google are scrambling, so a teenager in a basement can change the world.** (Vu Tran) Vu went through YC at 18-19 during the mobile run-up when Instagram sold for $1B; he says if he had a time machine he would have done a lot differently, and urges people not to have the same regrets. _[Y Combinator, Instagram, Google]_ — [2025-02-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0OopMnFAeoE)
- **Human-AI romantic relationships will be the next point of social controversy and will eventually be normalized like interracial and gay marriage.** (Vu Tran) About 150 years ago interracial marriage was illegal in many states, then gay marriage was contested; as technology improves, 'how can you fall in love with an AI' will go the same way. Jean adds a recent New York Times article about someone in love with a chatbot and recommends Ted Chiang's Exhalation on AI beings' rights. _[Exhalation by Ted Chiang, Westworld, New York Times]_ — [2025-02-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0OopMnFAeoE)
- **Hot take: the US is at least six months ahead of China on AI, partly because America is bad at regulating technology, which accrues an advantage.** (John Redgrave) Most Americans are scared of AI, China, or both, and do not realize weak regulation plus a talent edge keeps the US ahead. — [2025-02-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pAWpvZ2vn_Q)
- **Public speaking rule three: do tons of reps, and use ChatGPT as a mock interviewer to generate 20-30 likely questions, grill you, and grade your answers.** (John Redgrave) Redgrave prepped with former investigative reporters and then talked to ChatGPT on car rides; ask it to rate a response one to ten, or build a custom GPT trained on a media prep deck to tune each answer. The hosts praised this as the most actionable advice of the season. _[ChatGPT]_ — [2025-02-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pAWpvZ2vn_Q)
- **Each platform has a job: X for real-time information, Instagram for specific demographics like music culture, TikTok and YouTube as television, Reddit for deep obsessive information.** (Peter Pham) Pham calls himself chronically online by necessity and spends his time reading comments; the hosts agree that YouTube is television and creators are the new brands. _[X, Instagram, TikTok, YouTube, Reddit]_ — [2025-02-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rfDi_KmXq6Q)
- **We live in a post-truth, algorithm-fragmented world, so there will never be another Madonna or Michael Jackson-scale monoculture star.** (Peter Pham) Everyone's algorithm is different: nobody knew the number-one song, a friend had never heard Diet Pepsi or Bed Chem, and people now listen to 100+ artists and single songs instead of 10-15 artists and full albums. The remaining mega-artists like Taylor Swift built their base pre-Spotify. _[Taylor Swift, Spotify, Madonna, Michael Jackson]_ — [2025-02-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rfDi_KmXq6Q)
- **Hot take (held strongly but weakly): there is a good argument for nationalizing AI companies, because foundation model companies answer only to shareholders and the externalities of mass white-collar job loss fall on society and government.** (Jeremy Liew) A company is happy to replace a $150,000 salary with a $20,000 AI, but the cost of job loss isn't borne by shareholders; nationalization would internalize that externality. He expects meaningful impact within roughly a four-year horizon. _[OpenAI, Microsoft]_ — [2025-03-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=109Xypx-hM4)
- **AI will dramatically raise senior white-collar productivity and therefore eliminate entry-level roles, because junior staff were the historical mechanism for scaling a senior person's output.** (Jeremy Liew) Any job whose input and output are digital (analysts, L1 developers, law and accounting firms) is exposed, unlike fixing a car or doing surgery; this will propagate further as autonomous agents improve. — [2025-03-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=109Xypx-hM4)
- **China is 6 months to 2.5 years behind the US in AI but gaining faster due to more power, more data and greater talent density; US talent is dispersing across 10-15 foundation model companies and consolidating it could re-accelerate innovation.** (Jeremy Liew) Talent density drives innovation; people spinning out of OpenAI to start niche companies spreads it thinner. The hosts note the collective mindset of Eastern countries versus US individualism. _[OpenAI, China]_ — [2025-03-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=109Xypx-hM4)
- **The economics of frontier models don't add up with many duplicative players: next-generation models cost about $1B to train, then $10B, then $100B, and five companies at $100B each is $500B against a $600B annual US software market.** (Jeremy Liew) Foundation models are tightly clustered in performance with the most recent release leapfrogging, so the spend is largely replicated work; consolidating from 15 to 5 to 2 or 1 would remove duplication and align incentives. Historically training cycles were 18 months. — [2025-03-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=109Xypx-hM4)
- **What makes sense from an investor's perspective and what makes sense at the level of a country can give different answers when you zoom out.** (Jeremy Liew) He frames the nationalization take explicitly as speaking for himself, not Lightspeed, and as a thought experiment. _[Lightspeed Venture Partners]_ — [2025-03-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=109Xypx-hM4)
- **AI will revolutionize the world, but it exists in service of people; the future is humans plus AI, not humans replaced.** (Jonah Goodhart) Humans will still eat, interact and have dreams and goals; AI supports those. Jonah's current company Mobian builds an AI-powered contextual classification engine for internet content. _[Mobian]_ — [2025-03-10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UYtMgkz8brA)
- **Crypto is a petri dish of genius experimentation and fraud at once; neither 'saving the world' nor 'all scams' is right, and most things are shades of gray.** (John Wu) Wu says outsiders find it easier to paint situations black or white when reality is far more complicated; he hopes software-and-services companies like Ava Labs make other industries better. _[Ava Labs, Avalanche]_ — [2025-03-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VuBM_XODr7A)
- **Entire companies run on Type 2 experimentation: at Zynga every element on the FarmVille screen, down to sharp versus rounded button corners, was A/B tested.** Jean worked on every iteration of FarmVille; seemingly throwaway UI decisions measurably moved results, and treating them as reversible let the team move very quickly. _[Zynga, FarmVille]_ — [2025-08-04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NLxFGD_WaBw)
- **AI tools now do sentiment reading of video calls, effectively teaching people to attune the way some do naturally.** Read AI, founded by ex-Snap colleague David Shim and now over 4 million users, flags when meeting sentiment is high or low so sales teams can act on it. (Mentioned in a sponsor context but framed as an observation about technology.) _[Read AI, David Shim]_ — [2025-09-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TkXvlgPXitc)
- **Sports is more defensible to AI than any other industry because you can't prompt a physical skill and fans need human error.** (Paul Rabil) A robot hitting 100 threes in a row would go viral once and then nobody would watch; Jordan's dunks matter because another player feels pain. AI perfects human error elsewhere, sports requires it. _[Elon Musk, Michael Jordan]_ — [2025-11-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CU74FCSbrRE)
- **In a world of AI slop where anyone can be a podcast host, the value is in being a trusted curator who finds the signal in the noise.** The hosts read widely, talk to many people, and see themselves as curators of both content and voices, aiming to be the signal their audience trusts to deliver the right content at the right time. — [2025-12-15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M4HYavwtWzY)

### Guest life story & lessons (10)

- **Coming from poverty is a gift for a founder: when you know what zero looks like, you are not afraid.** (Vu Tran) Vu spent two years in a refugee camp in the Philippines and came to the US at age 3; 'how bad can it get? Turns out not that bad.' The hosts compare this to Bane's 'I was born in the dark' and note it inverts the assumption that only the wealthy can be fearless. _[The Dark Knight Rises]_ — [2025-02-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0OopMnFAeoE)
- **Seeing what 'good' truly looks like can tell you to change fields: meeting a 14-year-old Terry Tao on the Australian Math Olympiad team convinced Jeremy to leave mathematics for business, where the bar was lower.** (Jeremy Liew) He had assumed he'd become a math professor, but realizing how far he was from the standard of excellence, he finished his degree and went to McKinsey, then a decade of Web 1.0 companies (Netscape, AOL, Citysearch, IAC) before joining Lightspeed in 2006. _[Terry Tao, International Math Olympiad, McKinsey, Netscape, AOL, Citysearch, IAC]_ — [2025-03-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=109Xypx-hM4)
- **The failure he is most grateful for was getting fired from IAC for disrespecting a new boss placed between him and the CFO, a failure of self-awareness about how much he lacked in presence, judgement and breadth of experience.** (Jeremy Liew) As a 'snotty 28-29 year old' VP of Strategic Planning under Dara Khosrowshahi (later CEO of Expedia and Uber), he thought running spreadsheets was the job when it was a tiny sliver; he did not give Dan Marriott the respect he deserved and the role became untenable. It's hard to close the gap between where you are and where you need to be until you can see it. _[IAC / USA Networks, Dara Khosrowshahi, Dan Marriott, Expedia, Uber]_ — [2025-03-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=109Xypx-hM4)
- **The failure she is most grateful for was at 26 when she broke up with her longtime boyfriend and was rejected from Harvard Business School in the same fall, shattering her planned life.** (Michelle Huang) Her plan was HBS while he attended Harvard Medical School, then marriage and a white-picket-fence house in Boston; she ended up at Stanford GSB instead. The lesson was resilience and how to bounce back and change course. _[Harvard Business School, Harvard Medical School, Stanford GSB]_ — [2025-03-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fb8gYzg6ikg)
- **Advice to her younger self: don't assume that because something came easily once it will always be easy; life includes mountains you can't will your way over.** (Leah Wyar) She got pregnant the first time she tried in her mid-30s, then went through rounds and rounds of failed IVF for her second child and was depressed for a couple of years before giving up, then conceived her daughter naturally. Her gynecologist joked it was an 'egg exfoliation.' — [2025-04-28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yObnjfFejUI)
- **After the worst thing has happened to you, fearlessness increases, so doing brave things like a first date after six years of widowhood is 'part of the Wisdom Project'.** (Suzy Welch) She nearly cancelled the date, referenced Jack 33 times, and the man astutely asked whether she was ready to be loved by anyone besides Jack; she is not averse to trying again. Wisdom, she says, comes from what she did wrong, not right. _[Jack Welch]_ — [2025-11-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eRJyWu1CgI0)
- **Reaching the mission does not define you; the proudest achievement can be learning to embrace yourself.** (Jane Marie Chen) Embrace hit its goal of one million babies saved this year; Chen stepped down in May and now does corporate and individual leadership coaching teaching people to feel their feelings and step into alignment. _[Embrace, janemariechen.com]_ — [2025-12-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wL4BAe4lBAM)
- **Cultural literacy, being comfortable with people of any culture, is a blessing and a competitive advantage in business.** (Jim Keyes) A college experience in the UK taught him to embrace people who are different; his book has a chapter called 'Cultural Literacy'. — [2025-12-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wondntTp_cQ)
- **The Beijing government came to Dallas to ask for 500 7-Eleven stores before the 2008 Olympics, and China's speed of execution and transformation was stunning.** (Jim Keyes) Most companies begged for access to China; 7-Eleven had stores only in Hong Kong and Guangdong. Within five years Beijing went from more bicycles than cars to streets full of Mercedes. _[7-Eleven, Beijing Olympics]_ — [2025-12-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wondntTp_cQ)
- **Do not assume that what came easily will always be easy; life will hand you mountains, like infertility, that you cannot make happen by force.** (Leah Wyar) Leah got pregnant immediately with her first child, then went through rounds of failed IVF and years of depression for the second before conceiving naturally. She frames female biology as sometimes competing with career growth, a blessing and burden with no universal formula, survivable with work wives, therapy and resilience. — [2025-12-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KTlJZKNQRDc)

### Career strategy & promotions (42)

- **High-achieving women are trained to endure, not want too much, and wait their turn, so they underestimate their own readiness.** A 2020 Stanford study found women in leadership consistently underestimate their readiness for promotion even while objectively outperforming male peers; the problem is self-belief reinforced by patriarchal systems, not just opportunity. _[Stanford 2020 study on women and promotion readiness]_ — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **You can be disengaged in your dream job: reaching the title after 15 years of fighting for it doesn't guarantee fulfillment.** Jean became head of product for AR shopping and monetization at Snapchat, did less hands-on work than ever, yet was drained and disengaged, and couldn't admit it because of the title, pay and sunk cost of getting there. _[Snapchat]_ — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **Pursuing a non-obvious path (creating content full time instead of a prestigious VC internship) can look like settling to others but may be the bigger bet.** Jean questioned Cherie turning down a Bain Capital Ventures summer offer before Stanford GSB to test content creation; Cherie frames it as experimenting to see if it was a viable career, which it became. _[Bain Capital Ventures, Stanford GSB, LinkedIn]_ — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **You can dislike authority, rules and hierarchy and still thrive in a bureaucracy if you are willing to work within different cultures to achieve what you want.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers's wife points out the irony that he chose the Navy despite a strong streak of disregard for rules; he says he liked going to sea and adapted. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **When life feels overwhelming, 'fade' a little rather than taking yourself out of the race entirely, because re-entry in finance is very hard.** (Holly MacDonald) Finance has a high entry cost, so once you have a good role, hang in there even at less than 100%. Everyone fades whether they tell you or not; she recalls male colleagues who went to the golf store every lunch. She credits Dr. Laura Morgan Roberts' work on inclusivity for the idea of flexibility to fade. _[Laura Morgan Roberts]_ — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **Your past roles compound: the ideal next job sits at the intersection of two things you have already done.** (Andrew Yaffe) Four years leading NBA content strategy plus four years as head of business strategy launching new lines map directly to growing Dude Perfect across platforms and into new businesses; having launched the NBA's youth basketball competition, he knows every player in youth sports. _[NBA, Dude Perfect]_ — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **Your direct relationship with your boss is proven to affect your mental health, your financial outcomes and even your next job because references are so people-based.** The hosts frame this as why a toxic boss makes work '10,000 times harder' regardless of whether you are career-obsessed or treat a job as just a job. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **One in three people have left a job because of a toxic workplace or manager, making toxic bosses an economy-wide effect, and leaving is the right call.** From a recent BBC article citing 2023 research; Jean's reaction is 'good, get out of there, proud of you.' _[BBC]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Before reacting to a toxic boss, diagnose whether the problem is personality-driven or systemic, and temporary stress or structural culture.** From HBR's guide; everyone has bad days and deserves grace, but going to work every day with a tense chest not knowing what you will encounter is the sign of a pattern. _[Harvard Business Review]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **If the toxicity is one person, document everything while planning your next move: deliverables, deadlines and priorities in writing, specific feedback requests, saved emails and Slack messages, and visibility with cross-functional partners outside your boss.** Building relationships beyond your boss ensures your work is seen even if the boss undermines it. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **If the toxicity is systemic to the culture, skip lateral moves and start an exit: update the resume, restart coffee chats and reactivate your network to know your options.** A single toxic boss can be escaped within a company; a toxic culture cannot. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Look for a next workplace where work is meaningful and humane and employees are trusted, empowered and treated as partners rather than controlled by bureaucracy.** Drawn from Laszlo Bock's Work Rules; Bock is the former SVP of People Operations at Google. _[Laszlo Bock, Work Rules, Google]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **The strongest signal of a good future boss is that team members have followed them across companies; prefer someone who is not a first-time manager, founder or CEO.** Listener Isabel's exceptional boss was people-first, empathetic, led by example and had team members follow her to several companies; the hosts say first-time managers at startups are fine but carry more risk. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Boards recruit for a specific superpower; Mandy's is 15 years of digital consumer subscription, acquisition and retention experience.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Dara Khosrowshahi recruited her to Uber's board because it lacked digital consumer subscription operators as Uber One grew; Universal Music Group wanted subscription expertise for its Spotify relationship despite her knowing nothing about music. _[Dara Khosrowshahi, Uber, Uber One, Universal Music Group, Spotify, Barry Diller, Expedia]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Outside hires get paid more than loyal long-tenured employees, so compensation drifts out of whack unless leaders actively correct it.** (Mandy Ginsburg) This was the mechanism behind the accounting-department gap at Match; Jean adds that she only asked for a raise once, when people she had hired several levels below her were paid more than she was. — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Just ask: even if you get a no, asking plants in your manager's head that it matters to you, and squeaky wheels do get paid more.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Mandy's mother trained her to 'open up your mouth and ask' and often said no, so she is fine hearing it; she hates that squeaky wheels get paid more but says it is a fact. The worst case is rejection, so compare it to the best case. — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **The most reliable path to a public board is being a public CEO; otherwise target private boards that need a specific skill like HR, talent, or CFO/audit expertise.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Mandy teaches a women-in-boards course at Wharton and always gets asked how she got her seats; she distinguishes public boards, which want prior public-company experience, from private boards heading toward IPO. _[Wharton]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Once you have earned it, sell yourself as a package deal with a partner you are better with than alone.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Mandy and Shar Dubey joined private equity firms together as operating partners, telling firms 'you have to hire us together'; she says she would not have done this in her late 20s or 30s and had to slog it out first. The hosts relate it to choosing who they work with at their own company. _[Shar Dubey]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Bake optionality into your life the way VCs bake pro-rata rights into contracts: never have only one job offer or one school application.** Multiple options let you make an informed choice and have a BATNA. Apply to reach, target and safety schools; Cherie got into both HBS and Stanford GSB and turned down Harvard (while noting Goldman rejected her twice). _[Harvard Business School, Stanford GSB, Goldman Sachs]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **If PE buys your employer, expect cost cutting, new outside leadership from the firm's operators, and restructuring; as a customer expect higher prices and leaner offerings.** When Zimmermann was acquired by PE, Jean and her friends rushed to buy current-season pieces expecting quality to drop and prices to rise as margins were squeezed. _[Zimmermann]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **Mentors and managers are just people with faults; putting them on a pedestal early in your career sets you up for guaranteed disappointment.** Cherie notes that even someone great on stage with strong presence will disappoint if you expect them to live out a grand vision you built for them. — [2026-04-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PsDiVdzKIBo)
- **Take 30 minutes on a weekend to actually do the record-yourself exercise, ideally with a trusted friend's feedback, because no one gives you real-time feedback on how you come across.** Jean stresses people will not tell you that you seem closed off; Matt adds feedback from a trusted other matters because we do not perceive ourselves as others do. Simple posture fix: pull your shoulder blades down. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Run your life on a written to-do list for the week, the year and the term, rewritten every weekend from the prior week's reports.** (Rahm Emanuel) He carries a small card with the week's list, rewritten Saturday and Sunday after reviewing cabinet weekly reports, and kept lists per year and per term as chief of staff, mayor and now. Obama's departure gift was their framed 2010 to-do list signed 'not bad for a day's work'; the two walked the South Lawn daily to review it. _[Barack Obama]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Recession-proof your career by choosing a role that transfers across industries, like accounting, marketing or software engineering, and by deciding whether you want growth or consistency.** (Vivian Tu) Healthcare, energy, utilities and staples hold up while discretionary sectors like movies and apparel get cut. If you are single you might chase rocket-ship growth, but a head of household feeding others may want to pivot toward stable industries with smaller raises. 'Accountants rise up.' — [2026-05-04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ULlhVsNmU9M)
- **If you don't ask, nothing changes; if you do, something great can happen, so treat negotiation as 'what's the best that can happen' even if you hate confrontation.** Cherie dislikes confrontation but has learned to run toward the fire, since no one cares more about your career than you. — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **The biggest negotiation tip is that you never have to respond in the moment: keep a poker face, say 'great information, let me take it back,' and set a follow-up meeting.** Practice out loud in the mirror or with a friend for 10 minutes; hearing yourself say it once does something to the neurons, and business school even had a class on role-playing tough conversations like raises and promotions. — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Pick only three work tasks that matter each day; we are always more ambitious than our abilities, so big to-do lists guarantee feeling like a failure.** (Kofi Ampadu) Kofi typically gets more than three done, but once the three are crossed off he knows he had a good day. — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **If your values mismatch your organization's culture you go to work every day 'in a suit three sizes too small', so use your values to test whether you will last somewhere.** (Dr. Suzy Welch) People with very high voice (authentic self-expression) will find Goldman Sachs a poor fit, while high-achievement, high-affluence, low-voice people go into investment banking and don't mind. Young women often have voice ranked first with a 98% authenticity gap. _[Goldman Sachs]_ — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **Look for ways to convert seemingly irreversible type 1 decisions into reversible type 2 decisions, and take things off the binary.** An internship is a built-in mechanism that turns accepting a job or industry into a reversible trial. Cherie's late professor Joel Peterson taught 'take things off the binary': when something seems black and white, find the gray option in between. _[Joel Peterson]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **OKRs act as a guardrail: when everything feels urgent, written objectives let you decline requests that do not ladder up to your goals, but it is disastrous when your OKRs do not ladder up to your org's.** You can say 'I would love to, but I have to focus on XYZ first because that's my stated OKR,' pointing to a goal everyone has visibility into. Much of the value is in the exercise of figuring out which key results actually ladder up to the objective. — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Flip SBI around to ask for feedback: don't just wait for feedback, design for it.** Example: 'When I shared the product plan in the meeting, what stood out to you about how I presented it, and what was the impact on the team's decision making?' That is how you grow faster. — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **A pillar of the internet with a huge audience and no business is a rare open canvas, worth cutting a planned sabbatical short for.** (Jen Wong) After selling Time Inc. in January, Wong planned six to nine months off with a newborn but took only three; she wanted pure growth, had watched her editors at Time Inc. and PopSugar mine Reddit for trends, had been a long-time user, and became the company's only remote executive flying back and forth. _[Reddit, Time Inc., PopSugar]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Building a great career means helping others become a respected member of a winning team doing something meaningful, and if any one element is missing it doesn't work.** Peterson, who led organizations of hundreds of thousands, frames work as purpose, not just money or progression. The hosts note everyone wants to be respected and valued, and a good manager's responsibility is to build up the people on their team rather than serve themselves. _[Joel Peterson]_ — [2026-08-10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PHdNriPxQgk)
- **Do everything possible to avoid being in a lawsuit, because litigation consumes your mindshare for years.** Peterson's side lesson from his own suit: if there are threats of a lawsuit, resolve it before it becomes one; it is miserable and always in the back of your mind. _[Joel Peterson]_ — [2026-08-10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PHdNriPxQgk)
- **Behind the scenes LinkedIn is all metadata, so the more sections you fill out (experience, skills, recommendations, volunteering) the more you surface in recruiter searches.** Cherie worked on the profile team and says a recruiter filtering for someone in the Bay Area with ABC skills and certain experience will only find you if that data is registered on your profile. Goal: be discoverable. _[LinkedIn]_ — [2026-08-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qCAHgny6a4Q)
- **Most people, even Stanford MBAs with about five years of work experience, leave the About section empty; something is better than nothing, so start with one to three sentences.** Hands-up polls in Cherie's Stanford class come out 50/50 or 70/30 with most not having it filled out. Beginners should not skip ahead to advanced tactics. — [2026-08-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qCAHgny6a4Q)
- **Include quantitative figures wherever possible in your About and resume; concrete numbers root your accomplishments.** Examples praised: 'number one app', '20+ years in exec roles at companies of 1 to 25,000 employees', 'raised $10M+ of VC funding as a co-founder in my early 20s', even a 4.98 Uber rating. — [2026-08-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qCAHgny6a4Q)
- **Put your mission statement at the very top of your profile so readers know what you care about and why, not just where you worked.** Cherie's reads 'empower one billion people to advance in their careers and be the trusted voice women ages 13 to 35 turn to'; her professor calls it one of the best About sections he has seen. Ask yourself: what matters most to you and why? _[Glenn Kramer]_ — [2026-08-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qCAHgny6a4Q)
- **LinkedIn is a search engine, so naturally weave in the titles, skills, industries, tools and companies that genuinely apply to you rather than keyword-stuffing.** An advanced example dumps a list of Google, Facebook, McKinsey, Goldman Sachs, YC, Harvard etc. labelled as 'testing SEO terms for Sales Navigator'; Cherie says recruiters' searches scrape every profile field, but only include terms applicable to you. _[LinkedIn Sales Navigator]_ — [2026-08-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qCAHgny6a4Q)
- **Students and early-career people should fill their profiles with volunteering, publications and honors, and even babysitting belongs on LinkedIn because it shows responsibility.** The hosts praise a college student for using the volunteer, publication and honors sections and say putting what you have beats leaving it blank. — [2026-08-31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nu45PVvbkxA)
- **Even in traditional industries like banking, consulting or the military, your profile should show personality; the more you stand out to the reader, the better.** Cherie pushes back on 'I'm professional and don't want any personality', comparing LinkedIn to the interests section of a printed resume. — [2026-08-31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nu45PVvbkxA)
- **Good writing is learnable like jogging or piano, and better writing leads to better thinking and better opportunities.** (Glenn Kramon) Half his class admits to writer's block; his goal is to make improving fun. A Tiger Sisters appearance got him a South by Southwest speaking gig, showing that the medium of visibility is changing. _[South by Southwest, Winning Writing]_ — [2026-09-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6SIi_BQ0p2g)

### Networking, communication & influence (98)

- **Don't navigate existential decisions alone; use a personal board of advisors.** Both sisters keep friends they can call for hard questions and suggest treating the comments section as a board of advisors too. — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **As a subordinate, your job is not what you would do but what you must do to be effective with a specific boss, since everyone assimilates information differently.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) President Obama, a constitutional lawyer, read every prep document and arrived with three issues to focus on; President Trump assimilated verbally and visually, so Rogers distilled briefings to three takeaways with a map or picture on every subject, overriding his team's plan to change nothing during the transition. _[Barack Obama, Donald Trump]_ — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Know your audience is a universal communication rule, like changing your tennis game for clay, grass or hard courts.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers notes these are basic human dynamics, not unique to government: dealing with Congress differed from dealing with the White House, and you won't win at the highest levels playing one way regardless of surface. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **When you disagree with someone, ask them a question ('how did you think about X?') instead of saying you disagree; you either learn something or they discover their own error without being called out.** (Holly MacDonald) Learned from Bessemer's current CEO. Especially in big groups, questions rather than aggressive call-outs keep colleagues happier to work with you and build a team where people share ideas. — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **Real progress requires an inclusive strategy that meets men in the mediums they are in, rather than a 'politic of purity' that dismisses those spaces.** (Ron Busby) Ron says it's not women's responsibility to go on Twitch, but since men are 3-4 times more likely to die by suicide and are the ones most at risk of downstream violence, progressives must extend an olive branch and bring men into progressive agendas. _[Twitch]_ — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Women should build deal-sharing group chats the way men do: proactively send investment opportunities to a list of women and normalize saying no.** (Codie Sanchez) Sanchez's investor base has been 99% men who proactively asked to invest, so she now texts her deals to a list of women even though it felt uncomfortable. Her investment in Brett Adcock's Figure, now valued at $38 billion, came from a male friend sending the deal; she trades deals with Sophia Amoruso of Trust Fund VC. The hosts noticed at business school that men's group chats covered angel deals while women's did not. _[Figure, Brett Adcock, Sophia Amoruso, Trust Fund VC]_ — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Mirroring people's speech and movements out of genuine curiosity builds connection and gets more done; insisting 'I am who I am, conform to me' does not get you far.** (Codie Sanchez) Data shows people like you more when you mirror them non-creepily. The hosts identify leaning in and out of personas as one of Sanchez's superpowers; she prefers winning through connection. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **When negotiating salary, state your number and reasons in few words, then close with 'you can't blame a girl for asking', a smile and a shrug.** (Codie Sanchez) Her sales mentor Rob taught her the line; it is disarming because the boss cannot get mad at it, and it is a negotiating superpower men cannot use. Women have negotiating advantages they largely leave unused. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Say fewer things: a high signal-to-noise ratio signals intelligence and earns respect, so replace 'I was kind of thinking maybe' with 'here is what we should do, here is why, A, B or C?'** (Codie Sanchez) You become what you practice ('you don't get paid to be in the game, you get paid to practice; the game is the reward'). Deals are a zero-sum, immediate feedback loop on whether you communicated compellingly; if you are not doing deals, measure how often you get what you want out of life. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Live sports is America's greatest export and a tool for global diplomacy and human connection.** Jean raises sports as key to American cultural primacy; Yaffe agrees, citing the NBA commissioner's view and basketball at the Olympics bridging complicated geopolitical relationships. _[NBA, Olympics]_ — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **AI output needs human intervention because readers can now clock AI writing instantly by its tenor and patterns, and it gets you only about 63% of the way.** Tells: 'not this, but that' constructions, lists of three, rhetorical questions answered in the next sentence, identical LinkedIn formatting. Cherie says what she writes is better than ChatGPT and she must carry it to the finish line in her own voice. _[LinkedIn, ChatGPT]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Board seats come through relationships built over years, not through networking for its own sake.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Her first board, Care.com, came from a founder she befriended at a conference; the ThredUp seat came because chairwoman Patricia Nakache served with her on Care.com and they kept catching up every four to six months. _[Care.com, Patricia Nakache, ThredUp, IAC]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Men on boards bond over golf and shared university boards; women should create their own rituals, like dinner the night before a strategy session.** (Mandy Ginsburg) When she invited the women on Uber's board to dinner they said no one had ever asked; women skip relationship-building because they are caring for kids and parents and see it as inefficient, and she notes she never waits for a women's bathroom at board events. _[Uber]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Invest in relationships with people you admire because they make you happy, not to network; those are the ones that bear fruit in boards, advising and investing.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Write a list of the people from your first job onward you want to keep up with and ask what they will be doing in six months, a year, ten years; women are worse at this than men. — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Money language is social currency; learning a few key VC and PE phrases lets you feel calm and confident in any room without an MBA.** The episode is framed as a toolkit for work dinners, networking events and founder circles where terms like 'raised a Series B' come up; once you have the vocabulary you can ask follow-ups like who led the round and what the money will fund. 'Information is power.' — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **Map your social capital: know who in your network trusts you enough to vouch for you and make introductions.** This exercise comes from Stanford GSB's 'Paths to Power' class and mirrors how warm intros work in venture capital. _[Paths to Power (Stanford GSB)]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **Being easy to talk to comes from confidence, curiosity and charisma, expressed largely through non-verbal presence, which academics call immediacy.** (Matt Abrahams) Leaning in, open posture, eye contact and not being distracted signal 'I am here for you'; no single cue like crossed arms decides it, it is the amalgamation. He suggests studying what people you enjoy talking to actually do and building those behaviors into your own style. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Record yourself and review it three ways: watch without sound, listen without watching, then both together.** (Matt Abrahams) All Stanford GSB strategic communication students are recorded; it is like the dentist, unpleasant but worth it. Your voice distracts you from your movements and vice versa, so isolating each yields separate insight in about nine minutes. _[Stanford GSB Strategic Communication]_ — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Every business leader should have five stories stockpiled and ready to tell: value, pie-in-the-face, origin, crucible and solution.** (Matt Abrahams) The five come from the book The Pin Drop Principle. Stories move, motivate and inspire; his go-to is 'the biggest fight with my wife was over toothpaste' (she rolls, he squeezes), which builds curiosity and can pivot to respect, listening or knowing your audience. _[The Pin Drop Principle]_ — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **You can prepare to be spontaneous, like an athlete who drills so they can react in the moment.** (Matt Abrahams) Stockpiling stories, doing practice drills with AI, and having data or testimonials ready for a job interview frees you from being in your head asking 'what do I say', so you can be present. The hosts connect this to their motto 'prepare to win'. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Small talk should be rebranded because big things happen in it; it is the entry point to collaboration and deeper conversation.** (Matt Abrahams) Small talk was a last-minute add to the back of Think Faster, Talk Smarter and became the topic everyone asks about. He challenges listeners to notice that at least one close friend was met through small talk. _[Think Faster, Talk Smarter]_ — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **The number one small talk rule is to be interested, not interesting; it is hacky sack, not tennis.** (Matt Abrahams) Learned from matchmaker and academic Rachel Greenwald on his podcast: the goal is to keep the bag in the air, not ace the ball over the net, which takes the pressure off. Start with an inquiry or observation, e.g. 'I must have missed the memo on the blue outfits' at a buffet line, which led to a lasting friendship. _[Rachel Greenwald, Think Fast, Talk Smart podcast]_ — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Exit a conversation with the 'white flag': signal the last lap, then ask one more question or propose exchanging information.** (Matt Abrahams) Say 'this has been great, in a few minutes I am going to go talk to someone over there, but before I leave, one more thing'. Relying on biology ('I need the bathroom') can backfire, as when a man replied 'me too' and the conversation continued 20 more minutes. Research shows people feel conversations run too long because they do not know how to end them. _[Rachel Greenwald]_ — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Memorizing is bad for communication because you constantly compare what you are saying to the scripted version instead of being present.** (Matt Abrahams) He opens his Stanford class by telling MBA students the goal is to 'maximize your mediocrity': focusing on just doing it rather than doing it perfectly makes you do it better. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Improv is not about being funny; it is about being present, supportive and really listening.** (Matt Abrahams) He co-teaches with improv instructor Adam Tobin; Cherie recalls a Stanford theatre class taught by a professional clown that humbled business school students who could not do a simple game. _[Adam Tobin]_ — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Most communication is spontaneous, so use a structure; the Swiss Army knife is What? So What? Now What?** (Matt Abrahams) For a surprise project update: the information, why it matters to goals and KPIs, and what comes next. TV ads use problem-solution-benefit. Homework: after each episode say one thing you learned, why it matters, and how you will use it, to lay down neural pathways. _[What/So What/Now What framework, Think Faster, Talk Smarter]_ — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **A good apology names the behavior you did, not the feeling you caused, and specifies the remedy.** (Matt Abrahams) 'I am sorry I made you upset' is not an apology; 'I am sorry I interrupted you, that was disrespectful, in our next meeting I will paraphrase what you say before adding my contribution' is. What/So What/Now What packages it well; his book has a full chapter on apologies. _[Think Faster, Talk Smarter]_ — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Network strategically with a three-part goal: the information, the emotion and the action you want.** (Matt Abrahams) Be specific: what you want to convey and learn, how you want people to feel (curious, motivated), and the concrete ask (a LinkedIn connection, a reference). Unfocused networkers cannot assess whether it worked. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Do reconnaissance, reflection and research before networking, keep it in the back of your mind, then be fully present and follow up with gratitude.** (Matt Abrahams) Look people up on LinkedIn, read what they have written, listen to their podcasts; coming in hot with an agenda turns mentors off. The people who stick out are the gracious ones who follow up and connect their ask to what you have done. Cherie calls this 'biz rizz'. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **In any networking conversation ask 'what does winning look like for me' and also how you can help the other person.** Success in communication is not getting information out but making sure the other person receives it, so being other-focused and audience-centric is critical. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **On video calls fill half the screen (four fingers of space above your head), raise the camera to eye level, get good lighting and avoid the blurred background.** (Matt Abrahams) Attention is the most precious commodity so content must be salient; most people look like they are in witness protection in the dark ('find a teenager' for lighting). Recent research shows the blurred background makes people think you are hiding something, so use a real tidy background or an image. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Never confuse means with ends in a negotiation: give the other side the structure they want if you get the substance you need.** (Rahm Emanuel) In the balanced-budget talks, Democrats got Clinton's pediatric plus eye and dental coverage while Republicans got their preferred structure (a separate CHIP program rather than Medicaid). Parents don't care how the bill is paid, only that kids get care; 10 million kids were covered. _[CHIP (Children's Health Insurance Program), Bill Clinton, Newt Gingrich, Gene Sperling, Bruce Reed]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Before formal negotiations, exchange top-four priority lists with the other side and hear each other out, so you work up toward a win-win instead of starting at DEFCON 5.** (Rahm Emanuel) As mayor he met each of roughly 70 union presidents, each side laid out four priorities, then met a week later to respond ('I can live with one, two and three aren't happening, here's what I'd change on four'). Stating your number one tells the other side you'll compromise on two and three. _[Chicago unions]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **A damaged relationship can be rebuilt by hitting the reset button first and owing the other side the outreach.** (Rahm Emanuel) His first call on re-election night was to Karen Lewis offering a reset; because of the trust they built, the union later worked a year without a contract while they negotiated pension stabilization. _[Karen Lewis]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **More communication does not equal transparency; transparency means being honest and forthcoming, and the medium doesn't determine that.** (Rahm Emanuel) Every president tries to get past institutional media, and this one uses social media posts, but a president who is 'situational with the truth' isn't transparent no matter the channel. He would also ban social media for those 16 and under because algorithms shouldn't raise kids. _[Donald Trump]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Keep your profanity private; in 35 years of public life he has never cursed publicly because it is disrespectful, and performative cursing by politicians is inauthentic.** (Rahm Emanuel) He says you can be bare-knuckle in a private negotiating room, but the public standard is different; Democrats now cursing on camera is performative and 'either natural or not.' — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Being early is a constant and a form of respect; being hours late is disrespectful.** (Rahm Emanuel) He showed up 15 minutes early to his blind date and 20 minutes early to this recording; his big beef with President Clinton was that you can't be two hours late. _[Bill Clinton]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **When given a binary choice, a skilled politician takes it off the binary and reframes; as Kissinger put it, 'Does anybody have questions for my answers?'** Asked Chicago vs New York pizza, he picked Chicago but then named Tokyo as the real winner, which Cherie flagged as a lesson in reframing. _[Henry Kissinger]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
