- **Building something that changes people's lives is an addictive flow state, and after losing a spouse there is a fearlessness because nothing can scare you anymore.** (Dr. Suzy Welch) Welch gets daily DMs saying the Values Bridge changed someone's life and tells her kids 'I'm building something' when they say take a break; she also went on her first date six years after Jack died and found she referenced him 30 times. _[Values Bridge]_ — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **Design thinking means designing around what the user actually needs rather than what you think the problem is, because most products fail from solving the wrong problem, not from being badly built.** Invented at Stanford's d.school and popularized by IDEO (which designed the original Apple mouse), the five steps are empathize, define, ideate, prototype and test, watching what users do rather than what they say. At Snapchat, Jean's AR beauty-shopping team spent an afternoon in a beauty store on Abbot Kinney observing and interviewing shoppers before building anything. _[Stanford d.school, IDEO, Apple, Snapchat, Google, Gates Foundation]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Test and learn is a mindset: stop waiting to be perfect, ship a scrappy MVP, and let customer response rewrite your business plan, which is usually a fantasy anyway.** Taught in Stanford's Startup Garage and in The Lean Startup. Tiger Sisters itself pivoted from a reality-TV pitch deck to a podcast, and the first episodes were shot with one camera at a kitchen table with bad mics; waiting for a perfect set would have delayed learning what the audience wanted. _[Stanford Startup Garage, The Lean Startup, Tiger Sisters]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **The best MVPs often involve building zero technology and doing the work manually to prove demand first.** Dropbox released a three-minute marketing video and got 75,000 signups in one day before building the product; Zappos bought shoes from local stores and shipped them by hand; ClassPass matched people to classes with a spreadsheet; DoorDash began as Palo Alto Delivery with the co-founders taking phone orders and delivering food themselves. _[Dropbox, Zappos, ClassPass, DoorDash, Tony Xu, Palo Alto Delivery]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Jobs to be done: people do not buy products, they hire them to do a job in their life, and that job is usually emotional rather than purely functional.** Created by Clay Christensen at HBS. At LinkedIn, asking whether people update their profile for visibility, validation or to get hired determines what to build. Customers buy Sisters Matcha partly for the story of the small family farm in Wazuka, Japan, and the community, not just the quality. The exercise is to ask honestly what people hire you for at work: competence, confidence, calm, or personality. _[Clay Christensen, LinkedIn, Sisters Matcha, Wazuka, Japan]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **7-Eleven wins globally by localizing products through local licensees rather than running one menu like Starbucks or KFC.** (Jim Keyes) Japan has onigiri, Taiwan tea eggs, Korea kimbap, Sweden in-store baked breads. Keyes pitched an Americanized onigiri and a barbecue-and-coleslaw bento to his largely Japanese board, who looked at him like an idiot, but he still believes Americanizing onigiri is an opportunity, as pizza and Taco Bell tacos were Americanized. _[7-Eleven, Starbucks, KFC, Taco Bell]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **Regulation and cold-chain management, not lack of will, explain why Japanese-quality convenience food does not exist in US 7-Elevens, and the fix is working with authorities to modernize standards.** (Jim Keyes) US food-safety law restricts the butter content that makes Japanese bread soft, and US refrigeration must be kept around 35-40 degrees versus much warmer chilled temperatures in Japan, which dries out bread. Because temperature can now be measured electronically with immediate dispatch, companies could petition for slightly higher thresholds and better product. _[7-Eleven]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **Reddit chose advertising over subscriptions as its first business because ads scale with users and keep the platform open and frictionless.** (Jen Wong) In 2018 Reddit had some programmatic ads and a small subscription business; IPO was always the dream, but the company needed a business first. Ad platforms are very expensive to build, so you only do it if you believe you will be big; eight years in, Wong calls it one of the best bets they made. _[Reddit]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Use human analogs for how you show up on Reddit: it is like a dinner party, so add value to the conversation instead of selling.** (Jen Wong) Cherie's Stanford founder WhatsApp group asked how to use Reddit for product placement. Wong says founders can share expertise, do an AMA, or ask communities what they want, but marketing a product organically fails at scale because most community rules ban selling; to tableset the conversation, use the paid marketing platform. _[Reddit, Stanford GSB]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Reddit has outlasted MySpace, Vine, Clubhouse and others because it wins by being Reddit rather than mirroring what other platforms do.** (Jen Wong) Through mobile, video, social and now AI, Reddit kept growing as the only platform structured around communities where moderation and the business model both scale with users. It refused follower models, address-book dumps and identity-based ad targeting, so it is a consistent grower rather than a viral one, with quality maintained in tandem. _[Reddit, MySpace, Vine, Clubhouse]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **AI does not create a business out of thin air; you still need business know-how, grit and market intuition, and AI just gets you there faster with less capital.** (Kat Getzy) Kat used ChatGPT and Claude to research factories, write copy and automate systems for Physical Phones, so two to three freelancers did the work of six to ten hires. _[Physical Phones, ChatGPT, Claude]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **An accounts-receivable agent that reconciles a payouts database against a weekly bank export and posts late payments to Slack saves huge manual effort, but must still be double-checked.** (Kat Getzy) The bank is the source of truth; the automation has had snafus, so a responsible owner should not hand final financial say to an agent until it has been right time and time again without mistakes. _[Claude Cowork, Slack]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)

### Finance industry: VC, PE, banking, hedge funds (22)

- **Being privately owned with only one line of business lets an advisor sit on the same side of the table as clients, avoiding the conflicts that come from pushing products.** (Holly MacDonald) A public money manager's ultimate responsibility is to shareholders, so at some point goals conflict with clients; the hosts note many managers push their own funds and earn basis points on them, which undermines true fiduciary duty. Alignment lets the firm think clearly about how to help clients and pivot when needed. _[Bessemer Trust]_ — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **A search fund is 'fund my job search, but the job is CEO': investors fund a searcher's full-time hunt for a business and pre-negotiate the right of first refusal to fund the acquisition.** Targets are lower-middle-market SMBs, roughly a $5-30 million acquisition doing $5-50 million in revenue or $1-5 million in annual profit, where a new CEO can professionalize operations. The classic seller is a 70-year-old owner of a multimillion-dollar plumbing business with no succession plan. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **The search fund is a terrible model for the searcher and an incredible model for the fund, which owns 50-70% of a company the searcher found, financed and runs.** (Codie Sanchez) Sanchez teaches people to buy businesses for under $1,000 a month while keeping all their economics, because she thinks it is unfair to own part of a business someone must run for life just for teaching them; Wall Street wants the arbitrage and leverage without the hard work. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **A roll-up buys multiple small businesses in one category, centralizes HR, marketing, scheduling and software, gains supplier leverage, and earns multiple expansion, but it is not a beginner move.** Five rolled-up med spas can share one scheduling platform and negotiate Botox from Allergan down from, say, $5 to $3 a portion by buying for 100,000 patients instead of 1,000; the combined company gets valued at a higher multiple for being more professionalized and stable. Every problem of buying one business (culture, integration, compliance, 'why do these two locations hate each other') gets multiplied by five. Two of Cherie's Stanford professors rolled up traffic-cone logistics companies that serve highway construction. The hosts add that post-military MBAs are an archetype that succeeds as searchers because they want leadership again and can toggle between hourly blue-collar employees and the spreadsheets. _[Allergan, Costco]_ — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Venture capital is high-risk, high-reward equity investing: VCs buy ownership in startups rather than lending, winning big on hits and losing everything on failures.** Funding stages run pre-seed, seed, Series A, B, C, D and beyond into growth equity; Series A is typically $3-15 million. Airbnb raised a roughly $600K seed in 2009 and IPO'd 11 years later at around $100 billion. _[Airbnb, Crunchbase, PitchBook]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **VC firms specialize by stage and by industry, and later-stage investing requires deeper industry expertise.** Series A and Series E companies are evaluated with very different tactics. AI used to be an investable vertical 5-10 years ago but is now a horizontal since every company is AI-enabled; B2B SaaS (e.g. Salesforce) is a favorite category because software is highly scalable. _[Salesforce]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **VC is a power game built on signaling, social proof, access and warm intros, not just money.** Getting Sequoia, a16z or Lightspeed on your cap table signals to other funds to pile in; getting into Y Combinator is itself a signal, and Demo Day gives exposure to every major VC. As on Shark Tank, founders want specific investors for their expertise, not just the check. _[Sequoia, Andreessen Horowitz, Lightspeed, Y Combinator, Shark Tank]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **Private equity is defined by private ownership, majority control and a turnaround mindset: buy, improve, sell for profit.** Unlike VC's early risky bets, PE targets larger, bloated 'fixer-upper' companies, like flipping a house. Burger King, Neiman Marcus, Dollar General, Dunkin' and Dell have all been PE-owned. _[Burger King, Neiman Marcus, Dollar General, Dunkin' Donuts, Dell]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **PE firms make money on '2 and 20': a 2% management fee plus 20% carried interest on successful deals, funded by LPs, the firm's own skin in the game, and debt.** A $1 billion fund earns $20 million a year in fees alone; carry is where the real money is. LPs are endowments, pensions, high-net-worth individuals and other funds, who like seeing the GP invest its own money. — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **A leveraged buyout uses borrowed money to buy a company and the company's own profits to repay the debt, multiplying returns on a small equity check.** Buy a $1 million house with $100K down and $900K debt, improve it and earn $100K, and you've made 2x on your own money, like rent paying off a mortgage. But Toys R Us's $6.6 billion 2005 LBO carried $5.2 billion of debt, interest ate the cash needed to compete with Amazon, and it went bankrupt by 2017 with 30,000 jobs lost. _[Toys R Us, Amazon]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **PE gets a bad reputation for four reasons: layoffs, short-termism, asset stripping and sensitivity in industries like healthcare and education.** Cutting headcount is the strongest cost lever; hospital layoffs carry huge headline risk. Vice Media raised $1.6 billion including from TPG and went bankrupt in 2023; J.Crew and Payless closed hundreds of stores. _[Vice Media, TPG, J.Crew, Payless]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **Most PE exits are sales to another PE firm rather than IPOs; the goal is to hit a profit level and pass the company on.** Cherie learned this in a Stanford private equity class; different fund sizes buy different-sized companies and hand them up the chain. _[Stanford GSB]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **Not all PE is bad: Blackstone bought Hilton, expanded it globally, re-IPO'd it and made $14 billion without mass layoffs.** Outcomes depend on the firm's size, industry and turnaround strategy; many firms can improve operations and customer experience by cutting bloat. _[Blackstone, Hilton]_ — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **Professional investors exhibit groupthink too: the 13F filings of the biggest hedge funds show a huge overlap in the stocks they own.** (Imran Khan) 13F is the quarterly document every major hedge fund must file disclosing its holdings; Khan uses it to argue the market isn't more dangerous, it's just a new group of investors arriving as wealth transfers from baby boomers to younger generations who take more control of their own financial decisions. _[13F]_ — [2026-04-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AHOKAM47sS0)
- **Banks charge high overdraft and monthly fees because a basic checking account costs them about $300 a year to maintain, so a digital-first neobank with its own tech stack and no branches can save customers millions in fees.** (Jason Wilk) Younger, thin-file customers who cannot get a JPMorgan credit card are only profitable to legacy banks through fees; Dave's customers on average pay $400 a year in bank fees. _[JPMorgan, Dave]_ — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **Public-market investors are completely different from VCs: most VCs are looking for the exit door, so going public before building a solid public shareholder base leaves you exposed.** (Jason Wilk) Wilk does not regret the SPAC route but says the market fell out from under Dave as rates rose and growth stocks sold off before it had a foundation of public investors; the only frustration was timing. _[Dave, SPAC]_ — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **Simplify the profitability story to one clear metric, report progress against it every quarter, and the market will eventually reward you.** (Jason Wilk) With investor Imran's help, Dave told the Street it would be profitable at 2.1 million monthly paying members; when it hit that number the stock rose about 900% in a year. _[Dave]_ — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **When interest rates rise, investors write money-losing companies down to zero on fear of dilutive capital raises, so profitability becomes the only thing that matters.** (Jason Wilk) Growth at all costs was rewarded in 2021-2022; Dave pivoted to gross-margin improvement and contract renegotiation on an expedited basis without any layoffs. _[Dave]_ — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **Investors misjudge Snap by viewing it as a Meta-style social media company focused on ad revenue while missing its diversifying direct revenue and unseen Specs bet.** (Evan Spiegel) Snap has been transitioning from large North American brand advertisers to lower-funnel performance advertising with small and medium customers, a hard shift only Meta, Google and to some degree TikTok have pulled off. Its direct revenue business is over a $1 billion run rate growing 87% year over year, and investors cannot underwrite Specs until they try the next generation. _[Snap, Meta, Google, TikTok]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **Being an investor is a learning cheat code: every founder meeting, even one you will not invest in, accelerates your learning curve.** (Kofi Ampadu) Kofi joined a16z in 2021 when TxO (founded 2020) was still being built, so it feels like a startup within the firm and no two days are the same. _[Andreessen Horowitz, TxO]_ — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **Investing is about knowing the risk, so married or dating co-founders are a flag to de-risk with awkward questions, not an automatic no.** (Kofi Ampadu) No investor thinks anything is risk-free; the question is what happens to the company if the couple breaks up. — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **Keyes's biggest regret at Blockbuster was letting Carl Icahn talk him into joining as public-company CEO instead of taking it private with private equity.** (Jim Keyes) His plan was to partner with PE, take Blockbuster private, do the digital transformation and re-emerge public five years later; Icahn argued 'I'm the financial guy, you're the operator.' Had he taken it private, 100% of the debt would have been refinanced to come due in 2012 and the company would have sailed through the financial crisis. _[Carl Icahn, Blockbuster]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)

### MBA & elite education (10)

- **A liberal arts education teaches you how to think and analyze, and technical skills can be acquired later; a trendy CS major pursued without passion may be automated by AI.** All three are liberal arts graduates who went into technical fields. — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **The search fund was coined at Harvard Business School in 1984 but institutionalized and popularized at Stanford by Professor Irv Grousbeck, with classes and alumni investor groups built around it.** Cherie recently had coffee with Grousbeck, who is in his 90s, still teaching, and recently sold the Boston Celtics with his family; he pushed her toward entrepreneurship with the line 'I don't fear failing, I fear the regret of never having tried.' The 1984 innovation was having investors fund the search itself to match ambitious MBAs with retiring owners lacking succession plans. _[Irv Grousbeck, Stanford Center for Entrepreneurial Studies, Harvard Business School, Boston Celtics]_ — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Work several years before graduate school; life and work experience make you appreciate the education and give you standing to advise others.** Jean went to HBS after five years in tech (against Silicon Valley's anti-MBA advice) to found a company; Cherie went to Stanford burned out after the pandemic wanting to learn again. Rahm agrees: do not go straight from undergrad to grad school. _[Harvard Business School, Stanford GSB, Harvard Innovation Lab, LinkedIn]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **An elite school brand is an 'information shortcut' that can blind you and become a crutch; what ultimately matters is whether you can execute.** (Fernando De Leon) Brands like Harvard are shortcuts like Apple, and letting them take up too much of your identity can dull your scrappiness; Mr. Pullman did not care about his Harvard degree, only whether he could get it done. Getting in is valuable mainly as proof of discipline in pursuing a goal. _[Harvard, Apple]_ — [2026-06-15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sZFplGf0Y08)
- **Strategy at HBS is a roadmap for where to compete and how to win, and Porter's five forces is the classic tool for evaluating any company's landscape.** The five forces are threat of new entrants, bargaining power of suppliers, bargaining power of buyers, threat of substitutes and rivalry among existing competitors; Porter's framework was invented at HBS. They grade Starbucks on each: low threat of new entrants due to brand, supply chain and real estate scale; strong supplier power like Walmart and Costco; loyalty programs and the prepaid app reduce buyer power; expansion into pods and beans keeps substitute risk moderate. _[Michael Porter, Porter's five forces, Starbucks, Walmart, Costco, Dunkin Donuts]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **HBS teaches finance so that future CEOs can hold intelligent conversations with their CFO and board, not so every student can build DCF models alone.** The CFO is the expert and right-hand; the leader's job is to understand what is presented well enough to make decisions with confidence and represent them to the board. Cherie says she dislikes being in the weeds of models but loves adjusting projections with finance counterparts. _[DCF]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **Business school case studies should be updated to include a company's socio-political controversies, because customers and investors now factor those into everyday buying decisions.** Starbucks has faced public scrutiny and international and domestic issues over the last few years; the hosts stress they are not defending it but see it as a case in crisis management as well as business innovation. _[Starbucks]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **An MBA network has three layers a founder should use: classmates, faculty and professors, and alums who are often the investors.** Cherie's classmates were beta guests on her first podcast Cherie's Corner, Stanford lecturers Joel Peterson and Glenn Kramon appeared on it, and lecturer Allison Kluger advised her on media and entertainment. One classmate she interviewed, Chad Jennings, founded Gruns, which reportedly sold to Unilever for $1.2B within three years. _[Stanford GSB, Joel Peterson, Glenn Kramon, Allison Kluger, Chad Jennings, Gruns, Unilever, Cherie's Corner]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **The most important things learned at business school were the invisible soft skills for building trust and influence, not the hard frameworks.** Cherie spent over $250,000 on her Stanford MBA; you can hit deadlines, be a team player and over-deliver and still not be seen as a leader without these mental operating systems. _[Stanford GSB]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Each stage of education gave something different: Yale taught broad thinking about society, a Stanford engineering master's gave technical confidence, and business school gave business vernacular plus a lifelong sounding board.** (Jen Wong) Technical confidence means being able to dive into infrastructure and ML models and lead teams doing that work. Business school also gave Wong her first community of LGBTQ business professionals. _[Yale, Stanford, Harvard Business School]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)

### Leadership & management (61)

- **Integrity, doing the right thing, is the foundational value for high-stakes decisions, paired with articulating the why behind each decision.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers told his parents at age 11 he would be a naval officer, drawn by the moral and ethical dimension of leadership; he tries to explain his thought process and calculus so others understand the basis for decisions. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **If the only thing in your leadership kit bag is 'I'm the boss, do what I say', you are very limited; win hearts and minds so people become advocates.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) A leader's job includes building the bench for the future and setting culture, which requires communicating the what and the why rather than issuing orders. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Articulate commander's intent: when people understand the why and the end state, they can make good decisions in situations you couldn't anticipate.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) The military term describes giving subordinates the broader framework so that when facing uncertainty they can ask how the situation fits the goal and act; the sisters adopt the phrase for their own 'co-commander' dynamic. _[Commander's intent]_ — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **When there is no perfect answer, start with integrity, remember the mission and end state, and make sure everyone understands the broader context.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers acknowledged that what makes sense militarily may look different to the president, Congress or foreign counterparts, so he recommended based on the outcome requested and left political dimensions to political leaders. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Leaders must live the values they preach: if you tell your workforce to be inclusive and respect other opinions, you can't show up elsewhere declaring you have all the answers.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers says it would be stupid for a four-star admiral to lecture California tech leaders given his stated belief that he can always learn from others. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Business leaders now operate in the same environment the military optimizes for: high uncertainty, complexity, interdependence and rate of change with incomplete information.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Military lessons on culture, training, structure and process for high-performing teams translate readily to the private sector; figuring out how to optimize a company for that world is the great leadership challenge. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Selfless service, remembering it's about the mission and the people rather than you, matters more the more senior you get.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) What Rogers misses most is not being an admiral but being part of highly motivated teams that brought out the best in each other with a strong sense of right and wrong. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **People look up the tree, not down, so leaders set culture by what they do, not what they say.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Employees constantly watch what bosses do, say and how they got promoted; values that aren't lived come across as inauthentic and aren't adopted. Cherie jokes she applies the same standard to men she dates. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Never lie: disagreement is fine but honesty and directness are non-negotiable, and everything works better when everyone simply does their job.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Asked what line should never be crossed, Rogers names integrity and adds his belief that focusing on doing your job, without the emotional noise, makes the world work. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **A CEO's differentiated skill is identifying people who can do what she can't and unleashing them, not doing everything herself.** (Holly MacDonald) Trying to do everything yourself is a myth to bust for early-career people and would make you 'implode'. As COO she deliberately deepened in technology, operations and Gen AI processes, an area she wouldn't have listed as a top skill a few years ago. — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **Inclusive culture is not a warm fuzzy extra; it's where the best ideas come from, often from the person who joined two months ago rather than the 30-year veteran.** (Holly MacDonald) One mean comment in a group meeting can set an open culture back months. Junior people should thoughtfully step into gaps even senior people miss. The hosts argue this openness to input is why women are better investors. Bessemer's executive committee is at least half female. _[Bessemer Trust]_ — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **Diligence on an acquisition must include culture: interview the owner's top five to ten lieutenants, and contract the owner to stay on through a transition period.** Employees of a 10-100 person company may be excited about new management or resentful when a 'scrappy 28-year-old' replaces the founder, so you want the direct reports bought in before you buy. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Lead creatives with a few critical parameters, not mandates; be principle-driven and let the team design its own strategies in areas outside your expertise.** (Andrew Yaffe) Going into a creative offsite, his one threshold question is whether an idea can be a 30-minute video, since YouTube now rewards long content versus the 3-5 minute videos that thrived in 2009; he sprinkles in sport selection, geography and celebrity considerations without dictating. _[YouTube]_ — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **Keep the organization lean, make quick decisions, and never be the bottleneck, in contrast to stakeholder-heavy institutions.** (Andrew Yaffe) The NBA's many owners, players, teams and management made progress slow; he carried the opposite lesson into Dude Perfect. _[NBA, Mark Cuban]_ — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **Taking a founder-led company to its first outside capital and outside leadership requires interpersonal skill with the founders as much as strategy.** (Andrew Yaffe) Dude Perfect's five founders are in the office daily arguing about creative and business problems, unlike NBA players who are a step removed; talent management became his biggest learning. _[Dude Perfect]_ — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **Our founders are our brand, and our brand is our culture.** (Andrew Yaffe) The through line for the whole company; the hosts note the same is true for Tiger Sisters. _[Dude Perfect, Tiger Sisters]_ — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **Define your values explicitly and repeat them constantly so they scale beyond the founders as you hire.** (Andrew Yaffe) His first act as CEO was defining values; every all-hands opens and closes with them and highlights employees who lived them, because chasing shiny objects is no way to build a lasting brand. — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **Presenting calm and clear to your team while stressed inside is a real leadership skill.** (Andrew Yaffe) An NBA boss (a GSB alum) repeatedly said she had never seen him stressed, while he lives in a baseline state of stress; he calls his wife afterward. — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **A toxic boss is defined by recurring behavior that consistently harms employees' psychological safety, well-being, motivation and career growth, not by being stressful or demanding.** Citing HBR's 2024 guide to navigating a toxic workplace: gaslighting to avoid accountability, micromanaging, ignoring boundaries, and belittling or bullying in public meetings. Asking people to stay late for a deadline is not toxic. _[Harvard Business Review]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Public berating creates 'secondhand toxicity' and a culture of fear where everyone stops taking risks to avoid being called stupid.** Cherie watched a head of product at a young company pick apart a colleague's analysis in a 50-plus-person meeting, force him to do math live, and tell him the presentation and he were stupid; she likens the effect on onlookers to secondhand smoke. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **HBR's five red flags of a truly toxic boss: lack of self-awareness, lack of empathy, motivation by self-interest, inconsistent behavior, and taking advantage of power dynamics.** Self-interested bosses take credit and make risky attention-grabbing moves that earn them promotions at the expense of long-term team stability; the hosts joke the list reads like the personality disorders of sociopaths. _[Harvard Business Review, 5 Red Flags of a Truly Toxic Boss]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Companies that publish year-over-year gender-parity stats for leadership roles and make ERG participation career-beneficial for male allies create incentives that change behavior.** Cherie's tech employer had women-in-product ERGs with male allies and released annual leadership breakdowns by function under employee pressure; Jean, half a generation older, calls it a Disneyland she never saw. 'It's Barbie's world and we have Ken helping us out.' — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Great leaders protect their people from internal politics, public shaming and fear-based management, because people who feel safe collaborate and do their best work while people who feel unsafe self-protect.** From Simon Sinek's Leaders Eat Last; Cherie's managers would say 'CC me, I got this' when a senior person sent her a rude email, freeing her to focus on the work. _[Simon Sinek, Leaders Eat Last]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Good bosses take blame publicly and give credit publicly by name, which they can afford because they have built credibility in the org.** Cherie's boss Albert never singled anyone out when a project failed, consistently spotlighted team members' leadership on wins, remembered birthdays with team-signed cards and sent cream puffs when she hit 73,000 TikTok followers, creating a culture where a colleague DoorDashed her coffee on a bad day. _[Albert]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **'Am I the drama?' self-check for managers: Do people look nervous around me, is turnover high, are people hesitant to bring ideas, am I micromanaging, correcting people publicly, reacting emotionally to mistakes, creating urgency as a control tactic, or feeling threatened by high performers?** Follow-up questions: what is it like to work under me, do people grow, and if I cannot regulate my own emotions how can I regulate a team and grow a business. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Toxic behavior is fixable when the person lacks awareness rather than character; anonymous feedback, non-defensive apology and explicit expectations are the reform plan.** A leader on Cherie's broader team who cut people off and called ideas stupid in public, having grown up in an intense startup, greatly improved after anonymous feedback and became great to work with; some relationships, as in the Rachel McAdams film Send Help, are simply not fixable. _[Send Help, Rachel McAdams]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Even a highly analytical product company must remember the numbers are people whose hopes are up.** (Mandy Ginsburg) When a marketer complained that daily metric standups never talked about people, Mandy agreed: if the site goes down, those are people who might not meet. _[Match Group]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **A long-tenured CEO should ask whether they are still the person who will disrupt the company for the next 2 to 7 years.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Mandy worried 'old dog, old tricks' and wanted fresh thinking to keep Match self-disrupting, though she says she has a different view in retrospect. She had asked the board to make her and Shar co-CEOs and was refused, but ran it as a tag team anyway. _[Shar Dubey]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Her biggest asset as CEO was a team-sports mindset: genuine curiosity about people, loving to see them succeed, and the underrated skill of building community anywhere.** (Mandy Ginsburg) She played serious soccer at Berkeley and was never the best or fastest player; she says among any ten friends there is one who could be dropped anywhere in the world and have a community in 30 days, and she discovered she was that person only by doing it. _[UC Berkeley]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **A board member must be 'fingers out, nose in' rather than 'fingers in, nose in' like an operator.** (Mandy Ginsburg) A smart board chair gave her the phrase; operators want to say 'just do what I say' but management only listens and hopefully takes advice. The exception is a crisis like JC Penney's bankruptcy and CEO search, where board work becomes very hands-on. _[JC Penney, Uber, ThredUp]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Executive compensation is as much psychology as best practice, and must be aligned to actual value creation.** (Mandy Ginsburg) She has seen the people who drive the most impact underpaid and others make far more than they were worth; compensation is never perfect, so aim to get it mostly right and tied to shareholder return. — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Run a pay-equity audit because men pound the table and ask for money while many talented women never will.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Inspired by Salesforce's audit 13-14 years ago; a brilliant, analytical Asian immigrant product marketer on her team would never ask, so Mandy and Shar deliberately paid on contribution within bands. She ran the audit twice because she did not believe the equity result; the one anomaly was accounting, where long-tenured women were paid less than newly hired men. _[Salesforce, Shar Dubey, chemistry.com, match.com]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Hire people who are unlike you: an instinctive, creative decision maker should surround herself with analytical ones.** (Mandy Ginsburg) Team dynamic assessments at Match showed how few people were like her; in personal life she attracts contemplative introverts like her husband. — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Sometimes you should not dogfood your own product: to move fast, Snap's early ads team deliberately hid test advertisements in cities where Snap employees lived to avoid internal debate.** (Imran Khan) While building Snap's advertising business, employees would see direct-response ads and start email debates, so the team broke the ad algorithm in New York, Venice and other employee locations so they could test many advertiser types and learn the best ad stack. _[Snap]_ — [2026-04-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AHOKAM47sS0)
- **Mental shortcuts (heuristics) help you function but can stop you from noticing what the other person really needs.** (Matt Abrahams) After a meeting went badly a colleague asked 'how do you think that went'; Matt jumped into itemizing feedback, missed that the colleague's low tone and downcast eyes meant he wanted support, and it took six weeks to repair the relationship. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **Apologies are free, and a well-delivered apology deepens trust and has a strategic progression rather than being mere atonement.** (Matt Abrahams) Communication means 'to make common'; small talk, apologies and answering questions all build trust that lets people do bigger things together. Leaders should apologize when they err and reward others who apologize well, since businesses that try new things will break things. — [2026-04-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aTPhjLku45Y)
- **To lead a divided country, a president sets temperament, staffs from both parties, and refuses to sign social legislation that isn't bipartisan.** (Rahm Emanuel) He endorsed the Dignity Act on immigration because it has 21 Republican and 21 Democratic sponsors. He cites passing the Great Lakes Restoration Act with President Bush while simultaneously running the Democratic House takeover in 2005-06. _[Dignity Act, Great Lakes Restoration Act, George W. Bush, Nancy Pelosi]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **People are yearning for threads that tie them together and are exhausted by division; there is more consensus in America than in Washington.** (Rahm Emanuel) Evidence he cites: national excitement over the Artemis mission, the Olympics, people paying to sit in shared reading rooms, and rap and country stars crossing genres. A president has the bully pulpit to ignite that or pour gasoline on divisions. _[Artemis, Teddy Roosevelt]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **A leader must be 'multilingual': conversant in the boardroom, the break room, the family room, the classroom and the situation room.** (Rahm Emanuel) Clinton and Obama had different styles but both could connect with people where they are; he worries Democrats have 'spent four years in the bathroom.' The presidency is the only national office, so communicating to a wide public is essential. _[Bill Clinton, Barack Obama, Teddy Roosevelt]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Communication that people perceive as coming from your heart, consistent with your character, is capital you draw on in the low moments.** (Rahm Emanuel) During the dark valley of the healthcare fight, Obama simultaneously ordered the Afghanistan surge, with opposite coalitions on each; people who disagreed still trusted his motivation, which kept the two from derailing each other. _[Barack Obama, Affordable Care Act, Afghanistan surge]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Know the difference between the podium and the pulpit: great leaders find a spiritual connection with people in moments of anguish.** (Rahm Emanuel) Clinton went to Oklahoma City three days after the 1995 bombing, at a political low point, and spoke in a way Oklahomans understood; Obama led 'Amazing Grace' at the church in South Carolina after the shooting. The current president, he says, is 'all bully with no pulpit.' _[Bill Clinton, Barack Obama, Oklahoma City bombing, Emanuel AME Church shooting]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Local government is where people live, work and play, and five public investments determine whether a neighborhood succeeds: libraries, parks, transportation, schools and public safety.** (Rahm Emanuel) The mayor of Chicago is the most powerful mayoralty in the US, with schools, parks, transit, housing, police and the city council all reporting to the mayor. He wrote a book, 'The Nation City,' on this idea. _[The Nation City, Chicago]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **A published mission, vision, strategy and defined audience is what holds a company together when the stock collapses; a high stock price is a byproduct, not the goal.** (Jason Wilk) Dave's seed deck was about disrupting overdraft fees with no IPO timeline; when the stock fell 98% there was essentially no employee churn because the team was mission-aligned rather than purely equity-motivated, and they were still helping millions buy gas and groceries. _[Dave]_ — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **Being even-keeled, never too high on good news or too low on bad, is what gets a leader through a crisis.** (Jason Wilk) There was no 'I'll show you' mentality and no one to point fingers at in a macro-driven event with tens of thousands of investors; he just kept the focus on delivering the growth plan. — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **In a crisis, communicate from the fundamentals: customers only care that the lights stay on, and the board and team get a calm, collective unpacking of what is actually happening.** (Jason Wilk) No customer ever wrote in asking about the stock; the business was better six months after going public than before, so there was nothing to blame internally beyond margins. — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **Long-term investment is nearly impossible in public companies when average public CEO tenure is about four years, because profound technology shifts take decades, not a couple of years.** (Evan Spiegel) LLMs took decades of research, and the iPhone, seen as an overnight success, shipped only hundreds of thousands of units in year one on top of a decade of Apple progress. Snap has invested billions over 12 years in Specs. _[iPhone, Apple, LLMs]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **Loving the people you work with is life's true luxury; the ups and downs of building something are much less lonely with a partner.** (Jenna Ushkowitz) Jenna joined Ally at AAGC after having her second child, having admired the community Ally bootstrapped for seven years; they are now CEO and COO on Zoom for hours daily, and once competed for the same roles as 'phantom' rivals. _[Asian American Girl Club]_ — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Having a cause beyond yourself is what gives you the leadership and charisma to get others to follow, which is your operating leverage.** (Fernando De Leon) His motivation was always his family, then his five siblings and four children, and now the employees who have worked with him 15-20 years; a purely self-absorbed person cannot scale because they cannot inspire others to come along. — [2026-06-15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sZFplGf0Y08)
- **Leadership style directly shapes company culture, how performance is judged and how people grow, and ultimately shows up in stock price.** HBS's biggest pillar is leadership; the true value of the degree is said to be discovering your own leadership style plus the network. The hosts walk through four styles: servant, transformational, transactional and laissez-faire. _[Harvard Business School]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **Servant leadership flips the hierarchy so the leader serves the team, and it pays off in the bottom line because invested employees create better customer experiences.** Howard Schultz was among the first CEOs to give benefits to part-time baristas so employees could feel the third space too; Cherie's LinkedIn boss Albert gave her two miniature terracotta soldiers on promotion as a reminder that 'servant leadership is how we serve.' Military classmates and the book Leaders Eat Last embody the same idea of leading from the back. _[Howard Schultz, Starbucks, Leaders Eat Last, LinkedIn]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **Transformational leaders like Steve Jobs spark massive innovation and cult followings, but they need strong management teams behind them to maintain stability after they leave.** Jobs could look you in the eye and make you deliver the impossible; people lined up overnight for iPhones to get whatever he dreamed up. Elon Musk is the modern example. The downside showed when Jobs died and Tim Cook, an operationally focused leader, 'doesn't hit the same' even though Apple stayed wildly successful. _[Steve Jobs, Apple, Elon Musk, Tim Cook]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **Transactional (carrot-and-stick) leadership with clear rewards and penalties works in numbers-driven functions like sales and finance but creates cutthroat cultures at the expense of mental health.** Jack Welch's rank-and-yank at GE fired the bottom 10% yearly and lavishly rewarded the top 10%; Goldman did the same when Jean started, and banking, PE and consulting run up-or-out. Sales orgs 'eat what they kill' because they are judged within the quarter, while product and engineering work on multi-year projects that resist discrete metrics. _[Jack Welch, GE, Goldman Sachs, rank and yank, up or out]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **Laissez-faire leadership, where people self-organize around self-identified goals, unleashes creativity but only works with small, high-trust teams of hungry people who take extreme ownership.** Valve, maker of Steam, is the famous case. Cherie's big-tech experience is that without structure teams go complacent and 'rest and vest'; Jean argues it works for their two-person company and could scale to six equally trusted people. Zynga's 'be your own CEO' motto and independent studios each with their own GM were laissez-faire applied at scale. _[Valve, Steam, Zynga]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **Leadership style should evolve with company stage: laissez-faire for a two-person startup, transactional once you add people who need a rubric, then transformational once roles are clear and you aim for the stars.** No style is one-size-fits-all even within one company; the best leaders weave elements of each into a culture of their own depending on stage, industry and personal style. — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **Tim Cook handing Apple's CEO role to hardware chief John Ternus on September 1 is a live test of how much a CEO's temperament and style can turn a company's direction.** Cook moves to executive chairman focused on policy and global relationships; the hosts ask whether Ternus will be a stay-the-path executor of the Jobs/Cook plan or bring something different to product development and innovation, since 'the CEO is the captain of the ship.' _[Tim Cook, John Ternus, Apple, Steve Jobs]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **Cultural literacy is a career superpower: learning even a little of another language reveals differences that Americans otherwise misread as integrity issues.** (Jim Keyes) Keyes learned 'taxi cab Japanese' while steering 7-Eleven through Japanese ownership. In Japanese, 'hai' means 'I acknowledge you,' not 'I agree,' so Americans who think a contract point was agreed are shocked to renegotiate it the next day. He also studied abroad in the UK in college. _[7-Eleven, 7-Eleven Japan]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **Blockbuster did not die from missing technology; it died from cash management, and the Harvard case study gets the lesson wrong.** (Jim Keyes) Blockbuster had a billion dollars of debt coming due in 2009 when banks were shut during the 2008 crisis. In 2000 it partnered with Enron on streaming bandwidth, and in 2007 Keyes's first move was buying MovieLink, a streaming company built by five of the six major studios. He says the case belongs in the accounting or change-management course: for any entrepreneur, cash flow is oxygen. _[Blockbuster, Harvard Business School, Enron, MovieLink, Netflix]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **Giving an employee or an AI explicit success criteria seems stern but is actually kind, because it hands them the playbook; broad roles without it go off the rails.** (Kat Getzy) Most managers never articulate what success looks like, leaving employees and agents to fill in the blanks and do random things. — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Direct feedback is a sign of respect: clarity signals you think the person is smart and value their time, while fluff signals you think they are stupid, fragile or that their time is worthless.** (Kat Getzy) Kat's most common critical feedback in seven years of corporate was 'you're too direct', a trait women especially are told to soften; she role-plays a $1,000 credit overspend to contrast soft, hedging feedback with a clear version that names the issue, asks what happened and states consequences. — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Owning up to mistakes is non-negotiable on a team; the worst thing you can do is sweep it under the rug.** (Kat Getzy) Kat coaches people to talk through what went wrong without emotion, and says anyone who will not take accountability cannot work at CatGPT. _[CatGPT]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Say what you like and what you would like, never what you don't like, because employees who hear 'I hate this' go defensive and stop listening.** (Glenn Kramon) Start with praise, then the request; people who don't know your blunt style will route around you. In a performance review the sandwich bread is thick for strong performers, while for a troubled employee the middle dominates with concrete expectations and two-week and one-month check-ins. — [2026-09-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6SIi_BQ0p2g)

### Power, status & office politics (31)

- **Stay politically neutral in service roles: support whichever leader you work for to the best of your ability and leave political interpretation to them.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers served the last two years of Obama's second term and the first two and a half years of Trump's first term and never made political interpretations, which he saw as outside his role. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **High-level meetings follow a pre-defined flow with prep material and assigned speakers, much like business meetings the sisters call 'Kabuki theater'.** The sisters note that if you walk into a room not knowing where the conversation is headed you've already lost; Rogers confirms executive-branch meetings have defined issues, circulated viewpoints and a speaking order, though the chief executive ultimately asks what you really think. No electronics are allowed in. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **National security versus privacy is a false binary; both are valid imperatives and the work is mitigating tensions and deciding where one outweighs the other.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers rejected the either/or premise when testifying to Congress; Cherie raised the question from Keith Hennessey's Stanford GSB simulation class 'I'm Just a Bill', where she played a Democratic senator from Washington chairing the tech committee. _[Keith Hennessey, Stanford GSB, I'm Just a Bill]_ — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Arrogance doesn't preclude success; the world is full of arrogant people who have done well, in tech and in the military.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Some tech CEOs became friends whose insights he values; a couple he considered 'arrogant jerks', which he says is one person's impression. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **In male-dominated finance, listening well and asking good questions is a power move: accumulate information over time, then act at the right moment.** (Holly MacDonald) In a sales role covering mostly-male hedge fund managers, being female meant no macho ego competition, so she formed stronger relationships and heard what they really wanted. The hosts add 'information is power' and it only comes from active listening. — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **Patriarchy harms men too, and refusing to see men as victims robs them of agency and the capacity for empathy.** (Ron Busby) Ron says men are at risk of violence from systems they created; a 2025 Pew study found 15% of women and 16% of men feel lonely all or most of the time, so everyone is lonely but it is 'more dangerous when men are lonely'. _[Pew Research]_ — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Men now feeling looks pressure is the only sliver of hope for a 'post-looks' society, because expectations on women will never be removed on their own.** Cherie argues women have had toxic beauty demands since childhood (gel nails, hairspray, perfume) and 'if men had periods, pads would be free'; Ron counters that the goal should be a spectrum of gender performance where boys can wear makeup and grandma need not wear heels, not equalizing misery. — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **A woman buying from an older male owner should mirror the seller, show intensity, and redirect the conversation to profits and employees rather than invoking 'the patriarchy'.** (Codie Sanchez) When a seller said he could not present a woman in her 30s to his investors, Sanchez's move would be shock plus reframe: what matters is making more money and taking care of employees, which has nothing to do with gender. How you dress, talk and push back matters more for women; if a seller will never sell to you, move on to a 'perfect fit seller' where your background (for Sanchez, speaking Spanish with landscaping owners) is an advantage. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Pretty privilege is a usable strategy: the same woman earns about 30% more with makeup than without, so 'do you want to be right or do you want to win?'** (Codie Sanchez) Studies compare the same woman with and without makeup across service and professional industries, and being well groomed alone raises earnings; most men would do it for 30% more money. You decide your own line, but she will 'clown me up'. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Every man you work with is one of three archetypes, the three Fs (father figure, friend, or guy who wants to sleep with you), and you adjust your ask to the category.** (Codie Sanchez) With a father figure, ask for advice on how to negotiate so you end up on the same side of the table; with a friend, use a tongue-in-cheek 'would it be unreasonable if I asked' (a Chris Voss-ism) with a shrug; with the third type, play the tough card ('what are we doing here, are we going to lock this down?') rather than flirting. _[Chris Voss]_ — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Some toxic bosses publicly assign collaboration while privately instructing colleagues to sabotage your project, which directly damages your career and financial outcomes.** Jean learned years later, when she and a former peer became friends, that their shared boss told the peer in one-on-ones not to prioritize Jean's projects while telling the whole team in meetings to support them; she calls it 'House of Cards' behavior. _[House of Cards]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Political toxicity intensifies with seniority because individual contributors are proteges who reflect well on a boss, while managers competing for team size and visibility become threats.** Cherie, a senior PM, says her bosses shielded her; Jean, more senior, faced more 'Game of Thrones' land-grab dynamics. But listener stories show ICs get toxic bosses too. _[Game of Thrones]_ — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Society misconstrues narcissism as leadership, and empaths fall prey to narcissistic bosses because they give everyone the benefit of the doubt.** From listener 'Emma'; the hosts add that many business leaders are narcissists or sociopaths because charisma and owning the room get mistaken for management ability. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Toxicity in male-dominated fields often shows up as exclusion and inappropriate comments toward women.** A listener's boss said 'I'm just trying to figure out how to make you more likable' and asked what she wore under her scrubs; Jean's friend in a niche finance sector arrived to an empty office because every male teammate went to a baseball game without telling her, even though she is very sporty. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Senior women in male-dominated industries can be toxic to junior women because they were shaped by a 'one seat at the table' era where women had to fight each other for it.** A listener expected the only other woman on her team to be a default mentor and instead got a toxic boss; Jean says shedding a mindset built over 20-30 years is hard. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **HR is not the white knight; its function is to protect the company, not to save you from a toxic boss.** Jean says junior employees assume HR exists for them, but HR staff, however nice individually, have goals aligned with the company, so plan to save yourself instead. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Reverse ageism at work: don't grant credibility to the older engineer or MD just because they are older; build your own confidence so you can be peers.** Deference should be earned by someone proving they are legit, not by seniority in years; this varies by industry. — [2026-04-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PsDiVdzKIBo)
- **Going to DEFCON 5 unilaterally and backing a counterpart against the wall is a negotiation mistake that can poison a relationship for years.** (Rahm Emanuel) As new mayor he unilaterally pulled the teachers' 4% raise without consulting union president Karen Lewis, triggering the first teacher strike in 30 years; he won a longer school day and universal kindergarten but made his whole first term 'a living hell' and says the mistake is on him. _[Karen Lewis, Chicago Teachers Union, Chicago Public Schools]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **When nobody trusts your word, you cannot negotiate, and rebuilding degraded trust takes a generation.** (Rahm Emanuel) He contrasts de Gaulle telling Adlai Stevenson during the Cuban missile crisis to put the photos away because 'I have the word of the president of the United States' with today, where he says no ally believes what Trump says because he has four opinions a day on the same topic. _[Charles de Gaulle, Adlai Stevenson, Cuban missile crisis, Donald Trump]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **National security is a toolbox of four powers: military power, economic statecraft, political persuasion and cultural attraction, assembled differently per region.** (Rahm Emanuel) For Europe: move troops east to Poland and Romania, cut NATO HQ 10%, treat gray-zone attacks as a NATO response, negotiate a supply-chain and tech agreement. Asia: 'isolate the isolator.' Africa: a four-corner strategy around South Africa, Nigeria, Ethiopia and Kenya. He argues the current president has sidelined three of the four powers and relies only on the military. _[Joseph Nye, NATO, Mark Carney, Wall Street Journal]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Show up 'correct' and respect the office: Obama would critique men's attire in the Oval Office as a masked signal to come prepared.** (Rahm Emanuel) Obama's comments like 'your shoes need to get buffed' were criticism masquerading as suggestion, a way to take you down a notch and enforce respect for the office. _[Barack Obama]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **'Gold digger' is a tool to keep women down; every decision in life is a transaction and nobody calls men 'looks diggers' for the other side of the deal.** (Vivian Tu) Ambitious women get called power hungry, assertive women get called bossy, and no man has ever been called bossy, only a boss. Women should be loud about wanting financial stability, and she avoids relying on a man for money because whoever has the power to feed you has the power to starve you. — [2026-05-04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ULlhVsNmU9M)
- **Stop believing there is only room for one woman at the top; do not fight over crumbs, demand a bigger slice of the pie.** (Vivian Tu) On her JP Morgan trading desk the only other woman was her manager, 16 years her senior, alongside 30-40 white guys, and a new woman was hired only as one got senior. Women need to talk openly about pay, spending and what they pay employees, which gives everyone power. The hosts add their goal is not parity but an all-women top ten podcast chart. _[JP Morgan, South by Southwest]_ — [2026-05-04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ULlhVsNmU9M)
- **The persistent challenge for Asian American women is asking for more; the post-ask embarrassment fades with practice and no one can take your story or perspective from you.** (Ally Maki) Ally used to feel she sounded unhumble after standing up for herself in press interviews, but pushing through gets easier every time; Jenna adds that people ask because they want to hear from you, so speaking up matters. — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Know the game's rules are rigged against you going in, then win despite them while building allies, rather than complaining about rules you already knew.** (Kofi Ampadu) Kofi says it is no surprise founders of color are underfunded; you decide to play the game knowing that, play within the rules, and try to change them along the way. Treating the unfairness as a constant preoccupation is a distraction. _[Andreessen Horowitz, TxO]_ — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **You cannot change the system in your rookie year; earn credibility first, then use your bully pulpit to change the rules.** (Kofi Ampadu) Kofi compares a first-year player going to the league commissioner to demand rule changes: 'who are you, you haven't done anything.' Only as you make progress can you use your platform to make change. The hosts connect this to Rahm Emanuel's earlier comments on the bully pulpit. _[Rahm Emanuel]_ — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **Outsized sales incentives are the ultimate carrot: early Snap ad salespeople kept 1% of what they sold and top performers at LinkedIn and Zynga earned lavish reward trips.** LinkedIn's top 1% went to 'club' trips like Bali; Zynga's best-performing studio each quarter (60-120 people) went to Hawaii, Vegas or ski trips together. Jean says those glory days have since been clamped down on. _[Snap, LinkedIn, Zynga]_ — [2026-06-22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x9IXVlikKpk)
- **How you present yourself matters and is within your control, so refuse to be written off for something you can manage.** Jean wishes she had invested more in presentation earlier; being on camera makes it more important than ever, and they cite Codie Sanchez's 'paint me up like a clown, I'll make more money'. The hosts acknowledge the standards differ for men and women. _[Codie Sanchez]_ — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **Success is not about merit; it is about influence, visibility and alliances, and if you don't understand power you will lose to people who do.** From Jeffrey Pfeffer's Stanford class Paths to Power (and his book). Power is not title; it is who controls resources, influences decisions and pulls strings. Jean says she benefited from these dynamics unknowingly for the first half of her career and only started to play a part in the last three years as she got senior enough to see how the sausage is made. _[Jeffrey Pfeffer, Paths to Power]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Accumulate influence across sectors rather than titles, because power is not about being right but about having enough support and alliances to survive being right.** Sheryl Sandberg built a power map as chief of staff at Treasury, then as a cross-functional biz-ops leader reporting to Eric Schmidt at Google, so when she met Mark Zuckerberg at a 2007 Christmas party her web of influence was her most valuable asset. Carol Bartz, a highly capable CEO brought in to turn around Yahoo, focused only on tactics, never built allies, and was fired by phone call. _[Sheryl Sandberg, Eric Schmidt, Google, Mark Zuckerberg, Facebook, Carol Bartz, Yahoo]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Being excellent without visibility is a silent career killer, and you cannot ignore power dynamics even if, and especially if, you are brilliant.** These dynamics are dangerous because they are invisible and the people who understand them will not tell you, since they do not want to give away their edge. Exercise: map five people who will influence your next raise, role or deal, classify them as advocate, neutral or unaware, and connect with one this week. — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)

### Relationships, dating & marriage (83)

- **If you see a loved one settling, say it once or twice, from a place of concern, framed as a possible blind spot, not as distrust of their judgment.** Cherie raised concerns about Jean's last relationship only once or twice, knowing the weight of her words, especially since marriage is a Type 1 irreversible decision; Jean got engaged anyway but it ended, a 'close one.' _[Type 1 vs Type 2 decisions]_ — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **Of money, power and love, love is the only one you hope to keep your entire life; power and money go.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers, married 40 years with two sons, says love builds fulfillment and purpose that lasts. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Family isn't a choice you make, but a spouse is; getting that one choice right lifts you for life and getting it wrong is among the most draining feelings.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers tells his sons to take the time to find the right one for themselves, not for their parents. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **In your 20s and 30s, stay open to love and don't lose sight of it while building a career, because career-building has a price in time and attention.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers admits he focused too much on work yet met his wife in a bar, proving there's no one venue; when you find the right person, all your professional plans go out the window and you shift priorities. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Texting and posting images are not real communication; finding love requires physically interacting to learn what matters beyond looks, job and money.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers questions whether more contact with more people than ever leads to greater knowledge of one another, calling much of it superficial and meaningless. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **The most outrageous things done in the name of love can be self-love, like ending an eight-year engagement, or adventure, like moving to Brazil without speaking Portuguese.** Jean ended her eight-year relationship/engagement for self-love; Cherie jumped too fast into a relationship during COVID; Holly moved to Brazil for her husband and adopted a puppy rescued from Ukraine's front lines via Sochi Dogs. _[Sochi Dogs]_ — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **Be more discerning about who gets your love and trust; don't give your all to friends who have not demonstrated they can be trusted or reciprocate.** Cherie's biggest failure was falling into a repeated pattern of declaring someone a 'soul sister,' pouring in time and energy and introducing them to family, then finding it unreciprocated; she wants to protect her heart while remaining kind. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Male loneliness is everyone's problem because men express loneliness and dissatisfaction publicly as anger, while women are socialized to process it privately.** Drawing on Scott Galloway's conversation with Oprah, the hosts note anger is the most socially acceptable emotion for Western men, so sadness and loneliness 'funnel into Hulk smash', making it a public health issue; Galloway cites that four of five suicide deaths in a morgue are men. _[Scott Galloway, Oprah]_ — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Men bottle up emotions because vulnerability is not accepted, which creates a reinforcing loop that ends in anger, while women have friend groups to vent with.** Because showing a multitude of emotions is looked down upon, men cannot talk to anyone about feelings, whereas women 'commiserate in bathrooms together' and rely on stronger friend groups. — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Dating an avoidant, friendless man with 'machismo' energy shows how male loneliness plays out in relationships.** Cherie dated a man in her early 20s who had few friends, tried to one-up her guy friends, was repressed, gamed and streamed a lot; she notes anxious plus avoidant is a bad combination while the prefrontal cortex is still developing. — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Dating apps and other large companies have a financial incentive to keep people unhappy and disconnected, so male loneliness is partly a product of capitalism.** (Ron Busby) Research shows two men for every woman on Hinge, Tinder and Bumble; apps that charge $30 a month for visibility have no incentive for you to find a partner and leave, which feeds red-pill and black-pill logic that dating isn't worth trying. _[Hinge, Tinder, Bumble]_ — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Women can challenge their partners to build a 'more diverse emotional portfolio' rather than being a man's only source of connection and purpose.** (Ron Busby) Ron calls men's reliance on one woman for all connection a doom loop that collapses if the relationship ends; women can also model the utility of non-sexual, non-performative friendships with men. — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **We are in a relationship recession: for the first time in history there are more single people than ever, and the number of singles globally has grown by over 100 million in ten years.** The Economist reports that among Americans 25-34 the share living without a spouse or partner has doubled over five decades to 50 percent of men and 41 percent of women. Stanford sociologist Michael Rosenfeld attributes 13.7 million extra US singles in 2022 to the pandemic's reduction in dating. _[The Economist, Michael Rosenfeld]_ — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Underneath the shifting archetypes (housewife, hippie, girl boss, trad wife) there is one constant expectation of women: to be the nurturing mother, which is why 'having it all' is only ever applied to women.** A successful man just has to be successful; a woman must be successful, birth and raise children, and be a fabulous mother. Cherie is angry at the unfairness but grateful that financial freedom now lets women choose rather than stay stuck. — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Women's legal personhood and right to choose their own archetype is less than 100 years old, spanning four waves of feminism.** First wave (1920s-40s) brought voting, education and property rights; second wave (60s-80s, Betty Friedan's The Feminine Mystique) fought workplace discrimination and the assumption women are most satisfied at home; third wave (1990s-2010s) added intersectionality. Columbia University only admitted women in the 1970s, which is why Barnard exists. _[Betty Friedan, The Feminine Mystique, Barnard College, Columbia University]_ — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **The 'embarrassing to have a boyfriend' discourse is partly structural: over 50 percent of women now hold higher-education degrees versus 39 percent of men, so women who want to date at their level face a shortage.** A Vogue article from October declared having a boyfriend embarrassing and dominated discourse for three months. Cherie says she wants a partner who is the same or better than her in education and hobbies, someone she can respect and learn from, and the pool often does not meet the mark. _[Vogue]_ — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Most single people still want a relationship: across 14 countries only 40 percent say they are not interested, and 27 percent cite being too busy, too old, or unwanted.** Cited from an Economist study from the prior November, reframed by the hosts as a global and generational issue, with the 100 million new singles as a large pool of potential first dates. _[The Economist]_ — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **A relationship recession works like an economic one: demand collapses, confidence drops and people stop investing in their futures, with rising costs (emotional labor, therapy-speak, dating burnout) and capital hoarding (guarding hearts, bodies and time).** Jean, an econ major, frames it: in a financial recession borrowing is too risky, in a relationship recession emotional vulnerability is too risky. Situationships are 'microdosing vulnerability'. Choosing a candle, PJs and a movie over a possibly-nothing date is capital hoarding. — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Fix the recession with 'monetary easing': normalize low-stakes honesty so you communicate feelings in small doses instead of building up to one giant scary conversation, which beats ghosting.** Examples: 'I really like you but I'm scared' or 'where do you think this is going?' Small honest doses invite the other person to respond in kind; ghosting only adds to the problem. — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **'Fiscal policy' for dating means using your own power to build community, like organizing group dinners where each friend brings someone new.** Creating environments to meet more people, without the pressure of explicit coupling up, stimulates demand the way government spending does. — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **'Structural reform' means decentering men: celebrate Galentine's Day alongside Valentine's Day and treat platonic love as equally worthy of celebration.** Galentine's Day was coined by Amy Poehler's Leslie Knope on Parks and Recreation. Loving relationships come in many forms and need not be romantic or partnered. _[Parks and Recreation, Amy Poehler]_ — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Use 'pattern interruption', borrowed from tech behavioral design, to break your dating routine: swipe on people outside your type and drop superficial filters like six-feet-and-above.** Dating apps are built for shallowness. Cherie points to Charlotte and Harry in Sex and the City as a couple she would never have considered who became the happiest love of Charlotte's life. _[Sex and the City]_ — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Ask friends to set you up and go in blind, without researching the person, because social accountability improves matches and pre-research kills chemistry.** Jean is currently testing this: a friend connected her with someone, and she deliberately did not follow him back on Instagram to avoid preconceived notions, though she admits checking his LinkedIn 'for the facts'. — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Multiple things can be true at once: you can want a partnered relationship and still be fully empowered, single and powerful, or in an unconventional relationship, as long as you have autonomy.** The hosts note they are the first women in their bloodline to be single at this age and living alone, in a moment when that can still feel empowered. The romantic recession is counterbalanced by a resurgence of platonic relationships and community. — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Self-regulation of your nervous system is the crux of being in a healthy, secure relationship, because the nervous system is the lens through which you see and handle everything.** (Sabrina Zohar) Sabrina describes waking seven times a night checking for texts; a text meant she could breathe, no text felt like the end of the world. Conflict-repair should really be conflict-regulate-repair, since a dysregulated body thinks there is a tiger when it is really just a phone. — [2026-02-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=42BkJGOjQLo)
