Tom Brady walks onto the field confident because he studied game tape, and a pilot stays calm in turbulence because of simulation time; the more prepared you are, the more confident you can be and the better your chance of winning. _[Harvey Mackay, Swim with the Sharks Without Being Eaten Alive, Tom Brady]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **Preparation is not tricking your brain but using neuroplasticity to genuinely reprogram it with new patterns, including in relationships.** (Jim Keyes) The brain runs on preset patterns, some with fear built in, and can be rewired. Applied to love, this means learning to respond with your own agency rather than reverting to the same bad patterns and being pushed into reactions by your partner. — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **Relentless (not toxic) positivity is a good way to live because it keeps you focused on what you want to do, but faked positivity is toxic because it is not genuine.** Cherie says the world is already negative enough, so controlling your own positivity keeps you on track; Keyes agrees but distinguishes genuine positivity that comes from blocking out noise from faked positivity. — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **Americans are becoming lazier, not dumber: easy access to information lets perception replace perspective and critical thinking.** (Jim Keyes) Perception is taking something at face value; critical thinking, which separates humans from other species, is needed to determine truth rather than accepting what you read or saw on the news. — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **You are the captain of your own ship: analysis paralysis is a valid first response, but power and determination together can change your situation if you stick at it.** Cherie's takeaway from Keyes's full-agency story is that the episode is a reminder of the different ways to get unstuck, and that change should make you less scared because amazing things come from it. — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **The word liability can sound harsh; think of it as responsibility, and the point of the life balance sheet is awareness so you can change habits or decide how to actively live with each factor.** Cherie says her content-creation liability means she needs to change habits and lifestyle to account for the mental toll; Peterson's aim was never self-judgment but a fair, honest look at your life to optimize it. — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **Use mantras to rewire your operating system: what you think shapes what you say, and what you say shapes your life, so state the reframe of who you want to become and repeat it in moments of pressure.** Peterson, a self-described reactive, emotional person, adopted the mantra 'I am not my emotions' to say to himself when a meeting got heated. He was a believer in neuroplasticity. His four mantras: I have everything I need; it's not about me; I'm not my emotions; I am grateful. _[Joel Peterson]_ — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **Deep honesty about yourself lets you see where you are today, decide who you want to become, and engineer your internal dialogue to close the gap.** Jean observes the backward-looking exercises (family history, assets and liabilities) are traditional, but the action item, modulating your emotional state through mantras, is progressive and grounded in psychology; the whole class is about rewiring your operating system. — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **Every long career has incredible highs and lows, and you may have an entire bad decade; prepare to survive it and don't let it ruin you.** Peterson was sued by his former company in litigation that dragged on for around ten years, which he called 'a really bad decade'; out of it he founded Peterson Partners, now Peterson Ventures. Cherie at 27 was shaken that a bad decade was even possible, which showed her how long life is. _[Joel Peterson, Peterson Partners, Peterson Ventures]_ — [2026-08-10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PHdNriPxQgk)
- **Rain will fall on your fields, whether at work, in health or through a death in the family, so fortify yourself and your partnership before it happens.** The partner you choose is the anchor who gets you through the hardest times, tying the 'someone to love' and 'work to do' pillars together; the thriving gets posted on LinkedIn but surviving is the part nobody talks about. _[Joel Peterson]_ — [2026-08-10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PHdNriPxQgk)
- **Memorizing iconic texts builds inner strength for hard times, a practice Peterson follows himself and required of his children.** He cites prisoners of war who stayed sane by reciting memorized material; he knows the Gettysburg Address, Bible verses and French passages by heart. The sisters recall memorizing Chinese poems as kids and note the tradition spans cultures. _[Joel Peterson, Gettysburg Address]_ — [2026-08-10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PHdNriPxQgk)
- **Be wary of using AI for personal disputes because models reflect you back at yourself and hype you up, so you stop seeing other viewpoints; real friends who push back are more valuable.** (Kat Getzy) Kat keeps her AI use almost entirely in her business; hosts counter that you can ask for the bull and bear case or 'play devil's advocate', though you have to remember to ask every time and many users mistake sycophancy for objectivity. — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Write unexpected gratitude letters to people who helped you long ago, like a middle school teacher, because it moves them and empowers you.** (Glenn Kramon) His NYT piece on gratitude notes got the biggest reaction of anything he's written; AI cannot write one because it doesn't hold your memories. Examples: a pregnant counselor who drove eight hours in rain to a college orientation, a commander who dragged her desk into the company TOC, a violin teacher thanked for pretending to believe practice claims. Jean wrote hers to her fourth-grade teacher and a GSB recommender. _[New York Times, Stanford GSB]_ — [2026-09-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6SIi_BQ0p2g)
- **Turn bad outcomes into funny stories; a failed effort still yields material.** (Glenn Kramon) Former student turned comedian Lucas Zelnick taught him this; his unanswered Sergey Brin emails became a running class bit, and Cherie jokes she hopes Brin never replies so the story continues. _[Lucas Zelnick, Sergey Brin]_ — [2026-09-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6SIi_BQ0p2g)
- **Attaching a number or streak to a restorative goal, like 'sauna 10 times' or 'journal 30 days in a row,' turns relaxation into work and should be revised.** Cherie has done sauna once and swum zero of a planned five times; last year Jean strained her back attempting 'gym 30 days in a row.' The fix is to write just 'sauna' or 'journal 30 days total' as a reminder and track it loosely rather than as a pressured action item. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **It is fine to carry a goal over to next year if you still care about it, especially one that must be chipped away at rather than achieved overnight.** Cherie's sub-100 golf goal carried over from 2025; she has been taking lessons and playing and may carry it to 2027. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)

### Health, wellness & burnout (35)

- **Value drift is the slow disconnection from what matters to you, and its side effects are burnout, resentment and loss of identity.** Settling is a steady drift you don't notice until you're there, and it's hard to call out in friends or even in yourself. _[value drift]_ — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **Skipping the networking event to rest, read or run does not hurt productivity; your best ideas come when you step away from the desk.** (Holly MacDonald) She gets ideas for her team on morning runs with her dog; the hosts call it maximizing 'shower thoughts'. Jeff Bezos has said his most productive hour is walking around doing nothing in the morning. High achievers put too much pressure on themselves to give A+ every day. _[Jeff Bezos]_ — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **Reviewing last year's goals reveals patterns; Cherie's all go-go-go 2025 card taught her that 2026 must include rest and recovery.** The 'gym 30 days in a row' goal was not healthy and she hurt her back, so her 2026 card will include five massages and a Korean spa scrub; she writes the card in Sharpie and is taking weeks to finalize it deliberately. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Sweat equity builds the foundation, but a grind year needs to be followed by one that is more efficient and more fun to be sustainable.** Both sisters were near burnout by the end of 2025 from building Tiger Sisters; Jean's biggest failure was losing balance after the show took off in summer, culminating in crying at a celebratory dinner at Jon & Vinny's. _[Jon & Vinny's]_ — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Consistent therapy and daily journaling are the resources for working out your emotions and getting thoughts out of your head.** Cherie has done therapy consistently for over a year and plans to keep it and a morning journaling habit through 2026. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Loneliness is rising across the board, not just among men, largely because of technology and post-pandemic online habits.** The CDC reported rising loneliness in 2023 coming out of the pandemic, a 2024 Harvard study found 73% of people blame technology, and a Survey Center on American Life study found 15% of men say they have no close friends. _[CDC, Harvard, Survey Center on American Life]_ — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Social media provides 'social snacking': parasocial nibbles of interaction that never add up to a full meal of real connection.** Watching people's stories makes you feel you know them, but without synchronous in-person time exchanging energy there is no quality time and loneliness is never resolved. — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Many lonely men are withdrawing into solitary activities like gaming, streaming and pornography instead of trying harder to engage.** A 2025 article from Richard Reeves's American Institute for Boys and Men found men going on fewer dates, having less sex and spending more time on solitary and harmful online communities; Cherie half-jokingly counters with pottery, gardening, hiking, getting a dog, cold swims and therapy. _[American Institute for Boys and Men, Richard Reeves]_ — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **This is probably the first generation of men forced to navigate the self-hate and body dysmorphia society previously only demanded of women.** (Ron Busby) Men are going to Turkey for hairlines, chewing jawline gum and 'pinning' (anabolic steroids); venture capitalists are funding the Enhanced Games, a steroid-permitted Olympics in Las Vegas paying $250,000 to $1M for world records, which Ron says proves the agenda is economic and political. _[Enhanced Games]_ — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Group therapy and real-world commiseration with other men beat sycophantic AI girlfriends as an escape from loneliness.** (Ron Busby) Ron notes boys are talking to AI systems that are sycophantic by nature, and that the sisters' podcast itself functions as a form of group therapy with an audience. — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **The trad wife resurgence is a pendulum swing: women who ground themselves down in corporate girl-boss jobs in the 2010s burned out and now crave softness and the feminine.** The hosts recall day-to-night outfits and change-of-shoes bags of the 9-to-5 grind, and point to Ballerina Farm's Hannah Neeleman and Nara Smith as the new archetype of wanting to make sourdough at home. _[Ballerina Farm, Nara Smith]_ — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Regulating the nervous system means signaling to your body you are not in immediate danger, and practical tools include walking, shaking, the 'silent punch', ice cubes and sour candy.** (Sabrina Zohar) Intense cold triggers the mammalian dive response and the parasympathetic nervous system; sour candy works because the brain cannot ruminate and process a strong stimulus at once, which turns the prefrontal cortex back on. She uses the analogy of an office building where the executives (prefrontal cortex) go offline and children run the show; humming and Wim Hof breathwork also activate the vagus nerve. Meditation is useless when you are dysregulated because you must feel safe first. _[Wim Hof]_ — [2026-02-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=42BkJGOjQLo)
- **Inconsistent, unpredictable bosses are the worst kind because your body sits in a state of fear every day, similar to an abusive relationship.** Jean describes never knowing whether the boss will be joking or angry and mean on a given day, something you cannot control. — [2026-03-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SL9onG1uNb0)
- **Use AI as a low-cost personal trainer by uploading screenshots of fitness-tracker output and asking it to analyze the workout and build a multi-week plan toward a stated goal.** Cherie uploads Whoop/Oura/Garmin/Apple Watch summaries (calories, strain, duration) to ChatGPT; after aimless lap swimming it produced a structured plan (4 warm-up laps, 30-second rests, 8 at medium intensity) like a real swim coach would. _[ChatGPT, Whoop, Oura, Garmin, Apple Watch]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Dial workouts up or down in the moment by telling the AI your current state, such as four hours of sleep or an injury.** After straining her groin doing three days of yoga in Hawaii, Cherie asked whether she should snorkel and the tool told her to rest and recover. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Expert-level tip: upload InBody scan results (muscle and fat percentage per body part) to get detailed body-recomposition plans.** Both sisters have done InBody scans; the extra data makes recommendations far more specific, but only share health data you would be comfortable having leaked and consider blurring your name. _[InBody]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Use AI for nutrition from both directions: list what is in your fridge to generate a novel recipe, or ask at the grocery store for three healthy meal-prep recipes that last four days and get a shopping list.** Cherie does this weekly to use up produce; the tool also suggests substitutions (flaxseed for egg whites, cilantro for parsley). Adding 'must last in the fridge four days' avoids meals that turn mushy by day three. _[ChatGPT]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Photograph your meals in a single thread and ask AI to estimate macros, tally them by day, week and month, then build a nutrition plan from your real eating habits.** This replaces manual trackers like MyFitnessPal where you have to remember that one egg has 6 grams of protein; it also works for cutting sodium or sugar, e.g. if you are pre-diabetic. Jean notes you cannot spot-reduce fat in one body area. _[MyFitnessPal]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Upload a well-lit photo, ask in the third person how old the person looks, ask why it did not guess 25, then ask for non-invasive procedures, makeup and skin treatments to bridge the gap.** A friend gets 36-38 and is planning under-eye PRP based on the advice; asking in third person avoids refusals. Jean tried it and got 32-36, which is younger than she is, with a 'shit sandwich' explanation that she looks settled and executive. You have to be strong enough to take the blunt feedback, or instruct it to be nicer. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Send mirror selfies to get feedback on fit, accessorizing and makeup, and coax the tool into a 1-to-10 hotness rating and a path to 10.** The tool initially says a 10 is an unreasonable societal standard and must be pushed; a friend also photographs her finished makeup and asks how to look hotter with very little extra effort. The sisters are taking an in-person makeup class to pair with this. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Feed AI your week's commitments and workouts to optimize your hair-wash and blowout schedule around how sweaty you will get.** A friend calls it silly but the hosts call it genius: if a machine can optimize a schedule everyone knows exists, why not. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **After a color analysis, screenshot the available colors of a garment and ask AI which suits your season to stay consistent with your most flattering palette.** Jean was typed as a warm spring at a friend's bachelorette and says dressing in her season has been a huge unlock. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Talk openly about miscarriage: it is a common chromosomal outcome, especially when you wait longer, not a failure or a jinx.** (Sabrina Jalees) Sabrina lost a baby at 3.5 months after a failed IVF round and mentions it on every podcast because silence treats it like 'Bloody Mary'; she even objects to the word 'miscarriage' implying the mother messed up. — [2026-05-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OzsWwmX-D50)
- **Scheduling a C-section is a rational choice for the fear-driven or practical, but pregnancy itself can pull you into a 'motherhood Olympics' of wanting to do it naturally and perfectly.** (Natasha Leggero) Natasha scheduled her C-section to avoid her water breaking in public; Sabrina, who pre-pregnancy thought any 1% safety edge justified a C-section, found herself wanting to be a 'bathtub lady' once pregnant. — [2026-05-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OzsWwmX-D50)
- **Endocrine disruptors (non-stick pans, scented candles) may explain earlier periods and are worth minimizing at home.** Discussed when noting kids now start periods around 12; Sabrina's wife has thrown out such products and insists on wild over farmed fish, though Sabrina jokes ChatGPT told her farmed shrimp was fine. — [2026-05-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OzsWwmX-D50)
- **Sustainability, not balance, is the goal: weave friendships, hobbies and your life into the work so the second year does not burn you out like the first.** Jean keeps many hobbies (pottery, swimming, tennis) and says the first year of building Tiger Sisters felt really tough because of the workload. — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Trauma is not left in the past; it rewires the brain and nervous system and is stored in the body and limbic system, so you cannot talk your way out of it, you have to feel your way through.** (Jane Marie Chen) Reading The Body Keeps the Score by Bessel van der Kolk was a turning point. Talk therapy never worked for Jane because trauma lives in the emotional limbic system, not the thinking neocortex; she later 'stalked' van der Kolk until he became her therapist. _[Bessel van der Kolk, The Body Keeps the Score]_ — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **You cannot CEO your way through healing: instead of spreadsheeting modalities and pivoting fast, you have to slow down and feel the heartbreak, fear and anger you ran from.** (Jane Marie Chen) Jane bought a one-way ticket to Indonesia with a surfboard and attacked healing with founder intensity, keeping a spreadsheet of modalities and hunting for a 'magic elixir'. Healing only began when she slowed down with teachers, healers and friends who created a safe space. Cherie relates as a 'doer and fixer' who A/B tests solutions. — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **If you drive too hard in unhealthy ways you burn bright and then burn out completely; sustainable impact requires daily self-care.** (Jane Marie Chen) After shutting down Embrace, Jane had a complete mental and physical breakdown that took about a year and a half to recover from, discovering her mind could be that fragile after believing she was invincible. Cherie connects this to 'what got you here won't get you there': the next level requires letting go rather than white-knuckle grinding. — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **To start feeling again after years of numbness, sit in stillness and notice small physical sensations, since every emotion has a bodily correlate, and heal in community rather than isolation.** (Jane Marie Chen) Jane's memoir line is 'we cannot heal what we cannot feel'; at first she felt nothing, but patience brought sensations. Friends, therapists and teachers create the safety that lets the nervous system relax enough to access emotion. _[Like a Wave We Break]_ — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **Weave a daily or weekly practice of slowing down and introspection into your life, because constant grinding can be a way to avoid your own thoughts and eventually forces a reckoning.** Cherie admits being a go-go-go person for whom sitting on a beach with no distractions is scary; she takes from Jane that ignoring it too long leads to the kind of breakdown Jane suffered. The hosts suggest meditation, journaling, and talking to a trusted person or therapist, and ask listeners what their own healing journey would look like. — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **Stop promoting hustle culture and hyperbole on LinkedIn; honest conversation about the realities of building a startup is more valuable.** A post bragging about taking three days of maternity leave ('CEOs and CTOs, your code is your baby') prompted the hosts to say such content is unhealthy for the discourse. _[LinkedIn Lunatics subreddit]_ — [2026-08-31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nu45PVvbkxA)
- **Reviewing a goal board can reveal that you completed every activity that needed booking but none of the do-nothing goals you intentionally added to slow down.** After a go-go-go, career-oriented 2025 card, Cherie built rest into 2026 (relax at a spa, get a massage, sit in a park 30 minutes with no phone) yet those are the only unfinished squares, so her last 100 days are about chilling out. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **Make sleep goals measurable with a wearable, e.g. raise sleep score from 74% to 80% and move average bedtime from 2:21am to 12:20am.** Jean tracks with a Whoop; her average bedtime was literally 2:21am when she wrote the goal and is now much closer to 12:20am, though she hasn't hit the 80% score yet. _[Whoop]_ — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **Commitments to yourself must be calendared like a date with someone else, or work will always fill the time.** Jean's goal to 'go on dates even if it's with myself' has been neglected; Cherie prescribes reserving Friday or Saturday night, roughly 7-10pm, religiously off from work. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)

### Life design, goals & reinvention (75)

- **The 12-month projection test: if you continued exactly on this path for 12 months, would you be happy with where you end up? If you shrink from the answer, you're settling.** The sisters use this question across work, career and relationship decisions and call it their favorite framework; the new year is a crossroads where you either roll old habits forward or choose a new path. _[12-month projection framework]_ — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **Rate your alignment 1-10 in career, relationships and identity; anything under 7 is a yellow flag worth investigating.** Cherie does this roughly every quarter, asking whether life is in homeostasis and, if a score is 7, why, and whether the cause is in her control, out of her control, or seasonal. It doesn't need to be a long journaling session. — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **Three questions tell you it's time to move on: Does this version of me only exist for others' comfort? Am I becoming someone I admire? Would the woman I want to be say yes to this?** If the answer is no, you have your answer; these questions are scary but not to be shied away from. — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **Your zone of excellence can quietly trap you; being great at something others admire is not the same as being in your zone of genius.** Witherspoon was in her zone of excellence as an award-winning actor but not her zone of genius. 'Sometimes power means letting go of the thing you've built to build something bigger,' which Jean says describes leaving her 15-year corporate career to build the podcast having never been on camera. _[zone of excellence vs zone of genius, Reese Witherspoon]_ — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **Write a letter from your five-years-future self asking what she had to let go of to become who she is.** Cherie did a guided visualization of her future self (her house, her calm, her confidence) that made her burst into tears; the answers are already within you. — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **Stop settling with micro moves, not by blowing up your life: book an exploratory call, ask the uncomfortable questions, or spend an hour acting as the version of you who knows what she wants.** Opening the episode means your gut already knows; the worst option is knowing you're settling and doing nothing. Take a 5-10 minute step today for momentum. — [2026-01-05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xjw7AkB4dn4)
- **You can have it all, but not all at the same time: lean into career or family in different seasons rather than chasing perfect balance.** (Holly MacDonald) Holly took a lateral-or-down career move to follow her husband to Brazil and had her twins there, prioritizing family; now, taking over as CEO early next year with 12-year-old twins, is her moment to lean into career. — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **Most people never ask why they make money or where they want to be at 80, so put a calendar reminder to spend two hours once a year (or quarterly) answering those questions and setting goals.** Treat savings like a bill you pay to yourself; it's not possible to think about this daily during wealth-creation mode, so create guardrails. Holly, a philosophy major, cites Socrates: 'the unexamined life is not worth living.' _[Socrates]_ — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **The biggest mistake people make in January is rushing into the new year without reflecting on the previous one.** The hosts cite Harvard researchers finding that a few minutes reflecting on your performance improves the next round of performance by 18%. _[Harvard]_ — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **The stretch between January 1 and the Lunar New Year is a natural 'neutral zone' for slow, intentional planning, so there is no rush to finish goals by January 1.** They apply William Bridges' Transitions framework (the neutral zone comes after an ending and before a new beginning) and note Lunar New Year (Year of the Fire Horse) falls on February 17, giving a six-week transition window. _[William Bridges, Transitions framework]_ — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Pick one word or phrase of the year as the simplest focusing framework, since a long list of goals can be overwhelming.** Cherie's 2026 word is 'play' (her 2025 word in hindsight was 'grind'); Jean chose a series: fun, ease, joy, light, abundance and breakout. This takes 30 seconds to 3 minutes. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **A 5x5 bingo card of activities turns vague visions into tactical, activity-based goals that describe a full, balanced year.** Cherie's 2025 card mixed work and personal items: fall in love, make kimchi, host a dinner party, ski a new mountain, read 12 books, volunteer three times, gym 30 days in a row, speak at a conference; a vision like 'make the podcast a success' becomes a metric like hitting 100K YouTube subscribers. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Jean's '37 things while I'm 37' system: write a theme paragraph, a scripted average day, and categorized goals across health, relationships, career, family, growth, mindset, creativity, finances, personal care and fun.** Her 2025 theme was 'making big moves and believing everything will work out in my favor,' tapping the energy she had entering HBS ten years earlier; she tracked gym visits in 156 boxes and lists something to look forward to for each month. _[Harvard Business School]_ — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Use both written and visual planning tools because they engage different sides of the brain; start small and build the system up over years.** Jean pairs her multi-page written plan with an all-visual board; they stress you can do 10% of what they do, e.g. write down 10 or 15 things, and evolve it annually. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Use three end-of-year reflection questions: one word for the year, your biggest failure, and one thing to leave behind.** Cherie's 2025 word was 'grind,' Jean's was 'building' (and rebuilding herself after nearly a decade partnered, now single, an entrepreneur and in media); a longer question list is in the episode description. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Answer forward-looking questions as if the year has already ended: what was your headline, what resources did you need, and what did you say no to.** Both sisters' 2026 headline is 'breakout' and 'Tiger Sisters goes mainstream,' and they ask listeners to say it with them to manifest it. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Your power lies in the ability to say no, including saying no to yourself and your instinct to do a zillion things.** Cherie will say no to distractions from Tiger Sisters and to her own urge to travel every other weekend, and suggests a 'things I won't do this year' list as another exercise. — [2026-01-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4lZ8EsTExrQ)
- **Service creates purpose, and a lack of purpose is what many lonely men are missing; men can volunteer in ways that use their physicality.** (Ron Busby) During the pandemic Ron took PTO every Friday at 1pm to work at a food bank lifting heavy bags and breaking down boxes; though he went home to an empty table, it made him feel deeply connected to his community. — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **There are no silver bullets, but there are silver linings: no single product sold in an ad break fixes loneliness, so invest in structural solutions.** (Ron Busby) Ron closes by saying loneliness is not a permanent fixture of life and that thinking of it as alienation makes people more willing to own the process of progress collectively. — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **Celebrate non-traditional wins with your girlfriends, like passing the bar, a promotion or buying a house, since society only builds in celebrations for engagements, weddings and babies.** Cherie wants to try a TikTok 'celebration cake' with little flags for each win. Jean's friend bought a house in the Palisades on her own and the group celebrated it together. — [2026-02-09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4dHxBl2A2E)
- **Date (and do business) with detachment: detach from the outcome, not from your feelings, controlling only what you can and surrendering the rest.** (Sabrina Zohar) She has done nine years of daily work and is not done: 'if this is it, I don't want it.' You can't control other people, only yourself. — [2026-02-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=42BkJGOjQLo)
- **You should always be in your growth stage and can reinvent yourself at any time; if you can't let go, life will be very hard.** Cherie says she is a totally different person than 18 months ago; two years ago she was a 'corporate girly' running a product at Snapchat and would never have believed podcasting would be her main job. _[Snapchat]_ — [2026-02-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=42BkJGOjQLo)
- **Listen to your instincts about when to leave; people almost always stay too long, and the best time is when the business is going well and a successor is ready.** (Mandy Ginsburg) After nearly 15 years, a 2019 tornado destroyed half her Dallas house the week before earnings and a doctor's office 'fired' her as a patient over an unanswered breast-implant recall; she resigned in early 2020 with zero transition because co-leader Shar Dubey took over, six weeks before COVID shut the world down and dating boomed. _[Shar Dubey, Match Group, COVID-19]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Saying yes to too many interesting opportunities is a weakness; she planned a year of pasta in Florence and ended up on many boards instead.** (Mandy Ginsburg) She never aspired to be a board member or even CEO, joined her first board purely to learn what a boardroom is like, and now uses boards to stay abreast of technology and AI. — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Apply VC thinking to your life: take a portfolio approach and don't put all your eggs in one basket, including in dating.** Just as a VC wouldn't put the whole fund in one company, people keep 'rosters' to capture upside without losing it all on one bet. — [2026-04-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9cdiQonikNU)
- **The measure of a career is whether you left the world better; he points to concrete numbers from his time as mayor.** (Rahm Emanuel) 40,000 kids got pre-K and 40,000 more got full-day kindergarten; 20,000 students got free community college (tuition, books, transport) with a B average, plus 25-50% off at Illinois universities. Chicago schools went from 'worst in America' (William Bennett) to best of the top 100 (Stanford's Sean Reardon). He ties this to the Jewish concept of tzedakah: save one soul, save humanity. _[Chicago Public Schools, Chicago Riverwalk, William Bennett, Sean Reardon, tzedakah]_ — [2026-04-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ptYgxhdshaA)
- **Time is the only thing you cannot buy more of, so spend on a daily housekeeper and food delivery rather than status objects like Birkins.** (Vivian Tu) In the 'would you rather' round she picks a daily cleaner over 10 Birkins and food delivery over avocado toast, timing DoorDash orders to arrive as her workout ends. _[Hermes Birkin, DoorDash, Uber Eats]_ — [2026-05-04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ULlhVsNmU9M)
- **Even after hitting your FU number, do not retire; downshift from things you say yes to for money toward volunteer work, free speaking and teaching financial literacy.** (Vivian Tu) She would choose working to 65 and being extremely wealthy over retiring at 40 modestly, because she would be bored; getting to do something rather than having to do it wakes you up differently. A pipe dream is building a financial literacy curriculum for New York public high schools. _[Rich AF, Well Endowed]_ — [2026-05-04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ULlhVsNmU9M)
- **Be present by structuring work around the school day: kids are gone 8 to 3 once in school, so aim to be home by 4.** (Natasha Leggero) Advice Ali Wong gave Natasha: the first five years are hard, but afterwards you can build a career in school hours, be there mornings and from 4pm until the 6-7pm bedtime routine, then go out at night. _[Ali Wong]_ — [2026-05-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OzsWwmX-D50)
- **Don't let the dream of a child die because of the fertility bump or the partnership bump; solo parenting via donor or surrogate is a legitimate route.** (Sabrina Jalees) Sabrina cites Riki Lindhome, who had a child on her own, fulfilled her destiny without waiting for a man, and then fell in love with Fred Armisen. Both sisters say they want the full experience with a partner. _[Riki Lindhome, Fred Armisen]_ — [2026-05-18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OzsWwmX-D50)
- **America is shortchanging its future because government budgets use a 10-year window in which investments in young people look negative, even though over 30 years they are the most profitable investment a government can make.** (Evan Spiegel) Spiegel has been privately interviewing experts about the next 100 years in America, rooting the vision in founding values of freedom, equality and justice. Healthcare, preschool, food and housing for kids pay off over decades, but Congress's 10-year budget window hides that; he wants tools that show budgets over 30 years. He also argues the industrial-era education system built 100 years ago for factory careers should be redesigned, and that healthcare costs eating state budgets need an innovator's cost-cutting mentality. _[US Congress 10-year budget window, California state budget]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **No situation in your life is permanent unless you want it to be, whether you are in a great place or an unhappy one.** Jean says she barely recognizes her own life two years into Tiger Sisters after leaving corporate; things change all the time. — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **A clear mission grounds you against the fear of looking cringey: if everything is in service of the goal, who cares what people think.** The Tiger Sisters mission is to empower and uplift a billion women; they were once too bashful to say it out loud but now state it openly because it anchors every decision. _[Tiger Sisters]_ — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Boredom is a good sign it is time to change, and there are two kinds of scared: the 'something is wrong, get out' fear and the exciting, challenging scared that signals the next chapter.** (Ally Maki) Ally Maki says she listens to those feelings to guide her path; Jenna says when faced with two paths she picks the scarier one, meaning the one she has never done before. — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Reinvention requires letting go of seeing yourself as one trait or path.** (Jenna Ushkowitz) Jenna told her partner 'I'm just a comedy person' and it took a long time to accept she could do drama or start something like AAGC. _[Asian American Girl Club]_ — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Career paths zigzag and you can only connect the dots looking backward; during the lull or pivot it is terrifying.** The hosts note that Instagram and LinkedIn show only the shiny exterior, not the lulls in between. — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Reinvention is not becoming a different person but becoming more yourself.** Closing line of the live show, addressed to a room of people trying to become, return to, or rediscover something. — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Do the thing now; there is no perfect time, and 'not now' becomes 'not ever'.** (Kofi Ampadu) A friend sent Kofi a 2012 doc with his VC fund plan that had about 85% overlap with what he does today, and he felt it took too long because he detoured through consulting. He quotes a professor: the two most dangerous words are 'not now'. The hosts add that the best time was five years ago and the second best is right now, since life only gets more complicated. — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **Write your life plan down (in pencil) and email it to a friend for social accountability.** Kofi wrote his plan and sent it to his best friend, who years later showed him he had achieved nearly all of it. The hosts borrow Stanford professor Joel Peterson's 'write it down in pencil' because plans change but writing makes them real; Cherie's line: 'my biggest flex is I'm doing everything I said I would do.' _[Joel Peterson, Stanford]_ — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **Track whether you 'won' each day on three metrics: physical health, mental health, and completing three daily tasks, then roll it up weekly and yearly.** (Kofi Ampadu) Started in 2020 after a 2019 year-end review; Kofi logs daily in a spreadsheet with notes on bad weeks, posted weekly scores on Twitter (e.g. 'won six of seven days'), and compares year over year because 'what doesn't get tracked doesn't get measured.' He has kept it for five years and lets the data tell him to eat better or set more realistic tasks. _[Twitter/X]_ — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **Find work you love so much you would do it into your 70s; Kofi's father, a shop owner turning 70, is effectively 'retired' because he loves his day.** (Kofi Ampadu) His father, a former cab driver, started a mom-and-pop shop and still works happily; Kofi also points to Stanford professors teaching in their late 70s. Cherie says she wants to be an advisor, investor, professor or podcaster at 70 rather than retired and bored, at the intersection of what she loves, is good at and earns money from. _[Stanford]_ — [2026-06-08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6aXU9HjWnK8)
- **Answering 'what should I do with my life' requires excavating three data sets: your values, your aptitudes, and your economically viable interests.** (Dr. Suzy Welch) Welch's Becoming You course (800 students on the waitlist in one semester) synthesizes all three into purpose; knowing your values alone is not enough because you could want to be Celine Dion and have a bad voice. _[Becoming You, NYU Stern]_ — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **Eudaimonia (self-care, recreation, pleasure) typically conflicts with affluence and achievement, which explains the 'oh' feeling many young people get when they want both.** (Dr. Suzy Welch) Many students rank eudaimonia first with affluence or achievement right behind it; those values do not track together unless you inherited wealth. Jean says finance culture taught her to put self-care aside to succeed. — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **Four 'horsemen of dream destruction', all starting with E, pull people off their own lives: expectations, experience (the easy path), events, and economic security.** (Dr. Suzy Welch) Examples: taking the job your neighbor's brother can offer because it is easy, or staying in something after a layoff. Welch offers a free quiz to identify which horseman is taking your life; for Gen Z stability (the 'scope' value) matters more than affluence. _[Four horsemen of dream destruction]_ — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **People habitually live B or B+ lives for decades, and it usually takes a crisis like being fired, divorced or dumped to force the self-examination; do the work before the crisis.** (Dr. Suzy Welch) The outside world confirms your life looks like an A while you know inside it is not; Jean says her seemingly perfect couple and high-powered job was a B+ life she is now grateful crashed. Welch: you would otherwise be 45, crying in your car asking 'what have I done'. — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **There is no universal work-life 'balance'; figure out what YOUR balance is and give yourself permission to live it.** (Dr. Suzy Welch) Welch cites Jensen Huang saying 'I love my work-life balance, I work all the time'; rank ordering lets people admit they want fame, money and achievement with eudaimonia at the bottom and stop lying about it. _[Jensen Huang, Nvidia]_ — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **Reinvention comes with the territory because the economy, the world and you keep changing; you can 'die a thousand times' in a career and be reborn.** (Dr. Suzy Welch) Welch was fired from Harvard Business Review in her 40s, reported in the Wall Street Journal, refused a press release saying she resigned, and two weeks later Oprah's organization called. She started on manual typewriters, was an early BlackBerry adopter, and got her PhD in her 60s. _[Harvard Business Review, Wall Street Journal, Oprah, BlackBerry]_ — [2026-06-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Asm0bCXqd1s)
- **Use the 'whole body yes' test: only commit when head, heart and gut align; anything that produces contraction deserves a pause and the question 'do I really want this, to what end?'.** (Jane Marie Chen) A whole-body yes feels to Jane like an effervescent line of energy lifting up her body. On book tour she was offered three events in one New York day; old Jane would have done all three and been wiped, but she did one. The hosts joke that for overachievers the achievement is to not overachieve. _[Conscious Leadership Group]_ — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **Redefine success as living your values every day (love, giving, growing) rather than external outcomes, because 'success without fulfillment is the ultimate failure'.** (Jane Marie Chen) Jane quotes Tony Robbins and notes many outwardly successful people are empty inside, always comparing to the next person while the goalpost moves; values are the part you can control. _[Tony Robbins]_ — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **Use the Bezos type 1 versus type 2 framework: think slowly and strategically about irreversible decisions, but move fast and experiment on reversible ones, because treating every decision as irreversible slows you down and kills innovation.** Hiring your first engineer is type 1; changing a button color is type 2. At Zynga on FarmVille, Jean's team A/B tested every element on screen, down to sharp versus rounded button corners, because those were reversible. Startups die not from bad decisions but from slow ones. _[Jeff Bezos, Amazon, Zynga, FarmVille]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Most decisions people think are irreversible are actually reversible at a minor cost, so make your decisions faster.** Cherie's classmate got into both Harvard and Stanford, enrolled at HBS, declared in the first week that he had made a grave error, unenrolled, reapplied and ended up in her Stanford class. Dating is reversible; marriage is harder to reverse because of legal entanglements. _[Harvard Business School, Stanford GSB]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Use decision trees and expected value to make big life decisions: list options, sketch outcomes, assign a probability and payoff to each, and multiply.** Jean modeled a job offer that looked amazing on paper as best, worst and most likely cases and found it was a mediocre offer, while entrepreneurship looks risky but has huge option value and upside when mapped out. Netflix uses this to greenlight shows, VC firms to pick investments, and Amazon for pricing; gut instinct is cute but billion-dollar companies use math. _[Netflix, Amazon]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Run your personal life on OKRs: set an objective and two or three measurable key results per quarter, and share them with your team for support and social accountability.** OKRs were invented by Andy Grove at Intel and popularized by Google. On Cherie's LinkedIn team, people shared personal OKRs like going to the gym three days a week or learning piano, and teammates would tell someone to skip a late task to make their gym class. Example: objective 'improve my mental health' with KRs of three therapy sessions, journaling twice a week and one full rest day. _[Andy Grove, Intel, Google, LinkedIn]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **Do a 'life accounting': list your personal assets and liabilities, and write your family history, because you need to know where you are starting from to figure out where you want to go.** From Joel Peterson's sold-out Stanford class Engineering a Remarkable Life, which applied business principles to personal life. Assets can be financial, physical or personality traits; liabilities include student debt or being a caretaker for a family member. Class one was a one-page write-up of your roots. _[Joel Peterson, Engineering a Remarkable Life, Stanford GSB]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **The 'ten lives' exercise: when unsure about a big choice, ask in how many of ten imagined lives you would make it, which converts a yes/no into a probability.** Cherie learned it from her classmate Peter; for having children she would choose it in seven of ten lives (70% sure), and living abroad in two of ten. Jean reframes it as chapters of one timeline: her eight-year relationship with her ex-fiance was the right amount of time for the state she was in, and a new state calls for a different person. _[A Wrinkle in Time, Interstellar]_ — [2026-07-13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gxzHnCraPqI)
- **The three Cs work together: embrace change, have the confidence to act on it, and the clarity to execute; together they eliminate fear.** (Jim Keyes) Without clarity and listening you will not see change coming and will not have confidence, so any one alone is insufficient. The hosts note that distilling his philosophy into three Cs is itself a masterclass in communication. _[Education Is Freedom]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **To stay ahead of the curve as you age, engage your inner child, stay wide-eyed curious, and use creativity to defy the conservatism that creeps in with age.** (Jim Keyes) At 70, Keyes invests in space, advanced energy, AI and AR, and paints, sculpts and writes music despite never expecting to be good at them ('intelligence having fun'). Learning happens outside your comfort zone; there is no such thing as failure when trying something new. The hosts note he is even taking pilot lessons. — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **You can decide not to let old-age conservatism creep in, and your life education never has to end.** The hosts say Keyes has 'golden retriever energy' and the self-described maturity of a 12-year-old, doing brand-new things in his 70s, which makes them aspire to still be trying new things at 72. — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **It is very hard to architect a career; for most people it is a journey of discovery and reinvention that only makes sense in retrospect.** (Jen Wong) Wong never dreamed of being a business leader or a technical person and could not have predicted what she got from each experience; she holds out hope for another reinvention. The hosts add that you can reinvent yourself at any time in multiple chapters. — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **People spend enormous effort constructing a career but almost none engineering their life, so a remarkable life must be planned across career, family, relationships and children, not just work.** Engineering a Remarkable Life was taught at Stanford GSB by Joel Peterson, former chairman of JetBlue, head of one of the largest US commercial real estate investment firms, and a decades-long GSB professor and mentor who recently passed away. Cherie says it is not hyperbolic that the class changed her life and she references it daily. _[Joel Peterson, Engineering a Remarkable Life, Stanford GSB, JetBlue]_ — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **A remarkable life rests on three pillars, a person to be, someone to love, and work to do, and like a three-legged stool it collapses if any leg is missing.** Peterson insisted that whom you marry is one of the most important decisions of your life, unusual for a business school classroom, and even brought his wife Diana to class to answer students' questions. Part one addresses who you are; part two covers love and work. _[Joel Peterson, Diana Peterson]_ — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **Before your job, income or LinkedIn accomplishments, know who you are: your values, where you come from, and what you refuse to compromise on, because you cannot intentionally build a life alone or with someone else otherwise.** This is the first pillar, 'a person to be', and all the exercises in the episode are information collection about yourself, meant to give an honest, non-judgmental look at what you are working with rather than to pretend you are perfect. — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **Map your personal assets and liabilities: for each factor in your life (education, network, relationships, career, finances), write the asset side and the liability side.** Stanford GSB gave Cherie a network and learning (asset) but cost two years out of the workforce and student loans (liability). A family member brings joy and learning but may need financial care. Her tech-only network is a rich asset but undiversified when she needs advice in another sector. Her content career built professional reputation and opportunity but takes a real mental toll. _[Joel Peterson, Stanford GSB]_ — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **Values audit: what you spend your time, money and mind share on reflects your true values, regardless of what you claim to value.** Peterson's quote, which Cherie thinks about daily. Check your calendar for meetings, evenings and weekends; check where your money goes; notice where your mind wanders in an Uber or on a commute. Peterson joked that he claimed not to value luxury yet kept accumulating a garage of fancy old cars, so he must value them. _[Joel Peterson]_ — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **Track a representative week of your time without judgment, because what you do day to day shapes your habits and your habits shape your life and compound.** Jean suggests logging a week to discover, for example, that you think of yourself as fitness-focused but only went to the gym once. Cherie calendars everything, even dinner with friends, so for others, simply writing down at the end of the day what you spent time on works. Write three items each for time, money and mind share. — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **Always have a plan for your life, and it is okay that it is written in pencil.** A Peterson quote Cherie repeats constantly: she always needs a six-month, one-year or five-year plan, but so much changes outside your control that the plan must be erasable. _[Joel Peterson]_ — [2026-08-03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3125mje95W0)
- **A remarkable life rests on three pillars: a person to be, someone to love, and work to do.** Part one covered inward reflection on family history and values; part two covers constructing relationships and career. Jean notes HBS never taught anything like this as curriculum, while Stanford gave two credits for it. _[Joel Peterson, Stanford GSB, Engineering a Remarkable Life]_ — [2026-08-10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PHdNriPxQgk)
- **January is not the best time to reinvent yourself; the fall is, because back-to-school energy plus roughly 100 days left is the perfect window to recommit to goals.** Even if you are no longer in school, the seasonal 'sharpen your pencils' energy is renewing, and Q3-to-Q4 is a natural check-in to reignite, re-tackle or let go of goals set in January and go out with a bang. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **Five back-to-school tips for Q4: revisit your January goals, reset your physical and mental space, recommit to a routine, book yourself a future gift, and set aside time to nurture yourself in a 'cocoon'.** This episode covers tip one in depth; tips two through five are saved for part two of the series. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **Review personal goals quarterly the way businesses do, instead of waiting until year end.** Pull out your journal, vision board or bingo board and ask: which goals have I accomplished, which have meaningful progress, which no longer feel important and can be deprioritized, and which were actually mine versus created from someone else's expectations. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **A 5x5 bingo card of goals spanning fun, health, travel and intellectual growth is a balanced, visual way to picture a full year.** Cherie's 2026 card includes read 10 books (on book six), start a personal trainer (done), ski a new mountain, speak at a conference, attend a gala, host a live event, get a Korean body scrub, go to a concert and score sub-100 in golf; she leaves one square blank on purpose to fill later rather than rush something in. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **Ask whether each goal is intrinsic or came from an external obligation, and reframe it so it serves you.** Cherie's 'travel internationally' was fulfilled by a wedding and a work trip, obligations she was happy to meet but not her own itinerary; her next goal is to travel internationally purely for fun. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **Write a long list of 'N things to do while N years old' plus a one-paragraph theme, and count it a win if you hit N of them.** Jean's list for 37 runs to about 70 items across health, career, creativity/fun, personal self-investment, relationships, finances and things to look forward to by month. Her 2026 theme is 'investing in myself to create opportunities for magic beyond my wildest dreams.' — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **Some goals are simply too big and should be right-sized: 30-plus social events meeting new people is a lot, and a full split in 100 days may be injurious.** Jean has made progress on socializing via Venice events but 30 'sounds like a lot'; the sisters ask listeners whether Jean can learn a full split without injury in the next 100 days. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)
- **Homework: spend 20 minutes this week revisiting January goals without judging yourself, because the plan is written in pencil.** Take inventory of what worked and what didn't, then revise; it is okay to have a plan, but you have the ability to change it to what works for the next 100 days. — [2026-09-14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRfiOPxg9V0)

### AI & technology (56)

- **Governments now define national security with a significant economic security dimension, and policymakers must seek insight from those who will execute and be affected by policy.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) Rogers, who had only ever worked in the military, told Trump he lacked private-sector experience and so spent much time in Silicon Valley and Washington state at Obama's request to understand tech leaders' concerns and find middle ground. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **Government and Silicon Valley talk past each other because each thinks it understands the other; the real difference is culture, not talent.** (Admiral Mike Rogers) From 2014 to 2018 tech CEOs told him to stay out of the way; he told one CEO 'I'll put my engineers up against yours' and argued both workforces want to change the world, one through technology and one through security, so the simplistic good-versus-bad narrative doesn't help. — [2026-01-12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk56RJgqcFc)
- **The manosphere is a productized media ecosystem where pirates and profiteers sell men products between ad breaks, fueling an economy of male grievance.** (Ron Busby) Podcasts sell the carnivore diet, erectile dysfunction pills and testosterone; individualized algorithms push men deeper into an insular echo chamber that outsiders cannot access or appreciate. _[Manosphere]_ — [2026-02-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F783rcVaDqw)
- **In the age of AI, discernment is a survival skill: get smart enough to separate signal from noise rather than relying on experts.** (Codie Sanchez) An AI-generated 'Chinese monk' video with 3 million views claiming a feng shui reason there is no such thing as a lazy woman was being shared by her smart, accomplished girlfriends as real; she DMed them that it was fake. — [2026-02-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3fe7uZlKMI)
- **Follow your audience and lean into where consumption is going rather than fighting it.** (Andrew Yaffe) At the NBA he leaned into in-language content and vertical video early, redesigning the NBA app around vertical content five years ago; at Dude Perfect that means longer videos, since the algorithm is just a reflection of what people watch. _[NBA, YouTube]_ — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **Don't fear the algorithm: platforms are transparent that they optimize for more watching, so test and refine constantly.** (Andrew Yaffe) Because digital content is cheap to make, Dude Perfect posts different cuts across platforms to separate algorithm effects from content quality, and he wants the company to get even more analytical. _[YouTube]_ — [2026-03-02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Jy1d4_UQA4o)
- **Women are 25% less likely than men to use generative AI, and the people who learn to use it to their advantage are the ones getting ahead, so women cannot let this gender gap happen.** The hosts cite a recent Harvard study for the 25% figure and say it makes them mad; AI has replaced almost every Google search for both of them. _[Harvard]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **The number one axiom for using AI is 'garbage in, garbage out': the more accurate personal data you give it, the more personalized and useful the output.** Jean says this is the same rule they follow when building AI tools and training models, and it is why AI companies spend so much acquiring data sources. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Good prompts combine role-play ('act as my certified personal trainer'), specificity ('ask me 10 questions, then build a four-week program with warmups, form cues and modifications') and constraints (time windows, no heavy lifting, prioritize glutes and posture).** Jean's example: 'I have 25 minutes three times a week and do not work out before 9 AM or after 8 PM; build a plan that maximizes my time.' Role-play generalizes to life coach, social media manager and more. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **AI-generated exercise advice is roughly 90% accurate but only about 40% comprehensive, so without your own data the output is cookie-cutter and needs your discretion.** They cite a 2024 study in the journal JMIR Medical Education and say to apply critical thinking and reading comprehension to everything the tool returns. _[JMIR Medical Education]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Use dictation or voice mode to work hands-free with AI while cooking, asking for substitutions, oven temperatures or boil times.** Cherie talks back and forth with a British male voice she finds more authoritative, effectively using the tool as a sous chef. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Flip the prompt: ask the AI what you are missing and what else it should be asking you, the way you would ask an interviewer 'what question didn't you ask?'** Cherie uses this for creative gift brainstorming with parameters (friend got engaged in the redwoods, loves skateboarding and the beach, $100 budget). — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Offload life-draining but cookie-cutter dispute emails, such as airline and customer-service complaints, to AI so you can be firm yet polite.** In 2022-2023 Cherie used ChatGPT to complain to Hawaiian Airlines after an 8-hour delay at the Maui airport; her TikTok went viral with millions of views and international coverage from outlets like the New Zealand Herald and India Times. _[Hawaiian Airlines, ChatGPT, TikTok, New Zealand Herald, India Times]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **AI hallucinates confidently, so fact-check anything high stakes.** Google AI told Cherie she has a net worth of $2.8 billion 'confirmed by Forbes', which is false; they also caution that nobody knows how uploaded personal data is used. _[Google AI, Forbes]_ — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **AI is not a therapist and tends to be sycophantic, agreeing with whatever you say, so be careful when using it for emotional processing and wary of AI boyfriends and girlfriends.** Example: ask whether to ice out a man and it says 'yes queen', whereas a real therapist would suggest confronting him in a healthy way; human connection is the healthiest route, and companies are only now adding protections for teens. — [2026-03-16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4J3C-Rrzpo4)
- **Online dating grew because people stopped meeting at bars, churches and college; the first big audience was divorced parents with busy careers.** (Mandy Ginsburg) With a divorce rate over 50 percent, match.com's early-2000s audience was 'second time around' people with little kids; Tinder later brought in younger groups and the category proliferated. _[match.com, Tinder, Match Group]_ — [2026-03-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ifV2k4vcvrE)
- **Use AI to compare a company's filings year over year or quarter over quarter, because subtle language changes like 'may' to 'will' signal something worth investigating.** (Imran Khan) Read the first document fully yourself, then upload consecutive documents to an AI to surface incremental changes and new data points; he cautions that ChatGPT summaries can miss things hidden in a final line item, so still read the documents. _[ChatGPT]_ — [2026-04-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AHOKAM47sS0)
- **AI is a fast way to get basic knowledge of a business or any domain, but it must be followed by primary research.** (Imran Khan) Khan learned the basics of wine entirely through ChatGPT two years ago and then got better through tasting; the same applies to learning a business. _[ChatGPT]_ — [2026-04-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AHOKAM47sS0)
- **Use AI to track sentiment about a company across social media, Substacks and research, because the stock market is a voting machine and sentiment direction reveals consensus.** (Imran Khan) He does this manually now (e.g., pasting Reddit comments to see whether sentiment shifted positive or negative) and wants to build tools to automate it; he cites Warren Buffett's voting machine line. _[Warren Buffett, Reddit, Substack]_ — [2026-04-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AHOKAM47sS0)
- **Underwriting on cash-flow data instead of FICO scores lets thin-file customers get credit based on their paycheck and solvency between paychecks.** (Jason Wilk) Dave pioneered this in 2017 as an alternative to the 'unintelligent' flat $34 overdraft fee charged regardless of overdraft size. _[Dave, FICO]_ — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **AI models trained on billions of transaction data points (Dave's 'Cash AI') cut loss rates from 10-20% to about 1% while quadrupling average loan size from $50 to over $200.** (Jason Wilk) Hundreds of features such as where you bank, merchant types and even the time you buy food feed the model; loss rates have improved eight quarters in a row, and at nearly $2 billion originated per quarter every 10 basis points matters for profitability. _[Cash AI, Dave]_ — [2026-05-11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hxtgSnFdQ3E)
- **There are no major generational differences in what people want from technology; every generation fundamentally wants human connection and relationships.** (Evan Spiegel) Snap does not build for a demographic but invents new technology for self-expression and living in the moment; young people simply adopt new technology fastest. Content itself is social, a point of connection, so memes differ by friend group but the undercurrent is the same. _[Snapchat]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **Gen Alpha, as the first AI-native generation, can leapfrog older generations the way millennials leapfrogged Gen X by growing up with the internet.** (Evan Spiegel) Millennials became the people who set up the Wi-Fi router and taught their parents, accelerating their development; millennial parents now want their kids adopting AI tools to build, create and learn faster in a personalized way, and there is no better technology to accelerate learning than AI. — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **Rather than an arbitrary under-16 social media ban, governments should write clear rules and regulations for how social media is provided to young people.** (Evan Spiegel) A teen's readiness depends more on who they are as an individual than their age; Spiegel lets his 15-year-old use Snapchat because relationships are key to mental health, and research shows social media used in moderation correlates with well-being. He cites Snapchat design protections like double opt-in friending and no public friends list. _[Rahm Emanuel, New Zealand under-16 ban, Snapchat]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **The next computing interface after keyboard/mouse and the touchscreen is glasses that embed computing in the world, with see-through lenses as output and your hands and voice as input.** (Evan Spiegel) Spiegel frames every interface as an input plus an output: keyboard and mouse with a GUI, then the iPhone merging both into one touchscreen. Specs removes the screen entirely so you interact with computing the way you interact with a friend; Snap's mission with Specs is to make computing more human. _[Specs (Snap intelligent eyewear), iPhone]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **A future where everything is recorded and saved forever would be unhealthy for humanity because privacy is foundational to expression, creativity and democracy.** (Evan Spiegel) Snapchat was founded on questioning why everything online was saved forever when real life is ephemeral; permanent storage distorted relationships because people felt less comfortable sharing imperfect or intimate moments. Privacy is how you try out a bad idea without embarrassment; a fully recorded world suits an autocracy, not a healthy society. _[Black Mirror 'The Entire History of You' (the grain), Snapchat]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **Computing should not force you to leave the real world; AR glasses turn computing from a decades-long single-player experience into a shared one.** (Evan Spiegel) Spiegel loves the real world and finds it heartbreaking when someone stares at a phone at the dinner table; holding up a phone to view AR is like looking through a paper towel tube. Snap began working on Specs about 12 years ago so two people at dinner could build, play or learn together. _[Specs, Snapchat lenses]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **You can build an ad business in harmony with user privacy by targeting on context and intent rather than first-party identity data.** (Jen Wong) Reddit users are anonymous, so instead of collecting personal data, the platform converts the high-intent signals people give in communities (e.g. reading about vegan spices) into ad outcomes. Jean relates it to building affinity and lookalike targeting from AR ad work at Snapchat. _[Reddit, Snapchat]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Anonymity makes people reveal what they hide under their real name, which makes Reddit users incremental customers advertisers cannot find elsewhere.** (Jen Wong) Wong went through her whole first-car-buying journey on Reddit because she was embarrassed to do it with friends; conditions like illness or buying a car are universal human circumstances where demographics like gender barely matter. Cherie contrasts this with the posturing on LinkedIn, where identity and resume are attached to every post. _[Reddit, LinkedIn]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Reddit's quality comes from its community structure: each community has a purpose, its own rules and moderators, so conversation stays on topic and self-curates.** (Jen Wong) A succulents community is full of people passionate about succulents who are not there to talk politics. Reddit layers universal policies (no hate, harassment or incitement) on top of community-specific rules, whether a relentlessly positive curly-hair community or a debate space like Change My Mind. _[Reddit, r/changemyview]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Reddit has room for the full breadth of human experience, including loneliness and resentment, as long as discourse stays safe and civil under its policies.** (Jen Wong) Wong acknowledges a period in Reddit's 21 years when policies were not applied at scale and that perception lingers, but says the first thing Steve Huffman focused on returning, and her focus since 2018, was policy enforcement, moderator relationships, community tooling and brand safety; in her eight years she sees overwhelming kindness and helpfulness. _[Reddit, Steve Huffman]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **AI doesn't know anything unless humans tell it, which turned Reddit's trove of human conversation into one of its most valuable assets.** (Jen Wong) Reddit adds value from pre-training to post-training to grounding and search reference because it shows how people talk, stay on topic, and form opinions on products released yesterday. Reddit didn't build for this purpose; it built for its mission of empowering communities and making their knowledge accessible. _[Reddit]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Reddit's rise as a destination was timing, not design: its first 10 years were word of mouth, then the corpus got big enough to be indexed, and now 'reddit' is a top-16 search term.** (Jen Wong) People began finding Reddit via queries like 'best running shoes,' then started appending 'reddit' to searches, and the next incarnation is AI answer engines citing it. Cherie notes the shift from SEO to AEO (answer engine optimization). _[Reddit, AEO]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Once LLMs went from research projects to commercial businesses, Reddit needed licensing deals to secure value exchange and protect users' rights to deletion and privacy.** (Jen Wong) Reddit struck partnerships with OpenAI and Google that govern how data is used for training and as a reference resource; at the time nobody knew Reddit would become one of the most cited sources. Wong says the economy of the internet is changing so fundamentally that nobody knows where it lands. _[Reddit, OpenAI, Google, Gemini]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **Reddit aims to be the most human place on the internet, complementing LLMs' efficient answers with the validation people increasingly seek.** (Jen Wong) Users now literally post 'ChatGPT told me these were the best running shoes, what do you think?' or run the same query on Reddit to compare. Wong believes this triangulation behavior is becoming more common and will not go away. _[Reddit, ChatGPT]_ — [2026-07-27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-dANhTbV-JM)
- **AI is like a hundred mediocre interns, and you upgrade them by training them with tools, access and context like you would a human hire.** (Kat Getzy) Connect the AI to Slack, Google Drive, Gmail and calendar, and organize chats into projects so it accumulates context and learns your preferences over time. _[Slack, Google Drive, Gmail]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Graduating from chatbot to agent takes about ten minutes: download the Claude desktop app, turn on Cowork, connect your apps and ask it to do analyst-level work.** (Kat Getzy) Kat says agents are not 'witchy' terminal stuff; insiders have an incentive to make it sound scary so they keep the advantage, but demystifying it democratizes building companies and financial independence. _[Claude Cowork, Anthropic]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **An agent is simply an AI that can do stuff, such as crawling nine months of Slack brand-deal pitches into a Notion database and updating it every day at 9 a.m.** (Kat Getzy) Kat had Cowork parse messy Slack threads from her management team into a Notion database with company, dollar amount, deliverables and dates, then scheduled a daily automation to keep it current as a source of truth on inbound deals. _[Claude Cowork, Notion, Slack]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Scheduled automations can now be described in plain English inside the desktop app, replacing the fiddly data-mapping required by tools like Zapier.** (Kat Getzy) You click the scheduling button, describe what you want, confirm a couple of questions about tools, and toggle it on. _[Zapier, Claude]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **AI literacy does not mean knowing every tool; pick one platform, click every button and go deep, because the intelligence layer is commoditizing and the product layer is what differs.** (Kat Getzy) Kat calls the 'must know all tools' belief the most common wrong assumption; frontier labs are converging and copying each other, so learning Claude's voice deeply is a better bet. _[Claude, Claude Design]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Switching costs between AI platforms are overblown because you can ask a tool to tell you everything it knows about you and paste that into the next one.** (Kat Getzy) Every conversational tool is already summarizing and compressing your history, so no tool has perfect memory of you anyway. — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Professionals should pay for at least the $20-a-month pro tier of an AI platform, ideally expensed through work, because the jump in intelligence and access is significant.** (Kat Getzy) Kat and the hosts are on the Max plan, roughly an order of magnitude more expensive, which she acknowledges is not in everyone's budget. _[Claude Max, ChatGPT]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **When an agent makes an error, tell it directly: 'this was the success criteria, this is what you did, do you understand the delta, don't do it again.'** (Kat Getzy) Kat says to treat the model like a programming language: the more concrete and specific you are, the better, just as with an employee. — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Set up a dedicated folder per project before starting a Cowork session so the AI writes and reads its own memory (.md markdown files) there, and you can inspect and correct hallucinated logic in those files.** (Kat Getzy) The hosts compare this to corporate folder hygiene and to writing an onboarding doc for a new team member. _[Claude Cowork, Claude Code]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
