- **For creative or open-ended tasks, end your prompt with 'ask me any questions you have to ensure you have the context to complete this job' so the model finds the holes itself.** (Kat Getzy) Example: give 15 bullet points for a community-manager job description and let Claude interview you rather than racking your brain for all possible context; explicit success criteria matter more for unattended, repeated automations. _[Claude]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **The future is far less typing and far more talking to devices; dictation apps like Whisper Flow let you dump rambly context that LLMs can process.** (Kat Getzy) Kat says every desk in SF startups now has a gooseneck mic and everyone is whispering to their AI; she talks to agents hands-free in the car for brainstorming and nerdy rabbit holes like how billionaires borrow against assets. _[Whisper Flow, ChatGPT]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **For voice learning sessions, append 'give me the response as a transcript that could be read aloud' and hit play, because the default voice mode is too short and header-heavy answers make no sense read aloud.** (Kat Getzy) Jean uses voice mode to practice Mandarin and Hong Kong Cantonese, and notes you can ask it to slow down. _[ChatGPT]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Claude pushes back by default more than ChatGPT, which will hype you up no matter what.** (Kat Getzy) The hosts liken ChatGPT's default to a friend who always empathizes unless you explicitly ask for solutions or opposing views. _[Claude, ChatGPT]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **The honest position on AI's environmental impact is that nobody has reliable numbers, with sources ranging from 'scourge of the earth' to 'less than a hair dryer'.** (Kat Getzy) Kat feels guilty not because she knows it is bad but because she does not know the impact; she submitted this as her question to Anthropic's 'Hard Questions' campaign and suspects the impact is overblown but cannot prove it. _[Anthropic Hard Questions campaign]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **When an AI says it cannot do something, push back with 'yes you can, try harder' and it often finds a creative workaround.** (Kat Getzy) Claude claimed it could not read video, then split the video into screenshots every five seconds and inferred content from frames and captions. _[Claude]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **There is no AI playbook yet, so the best learning comes from friends experimenting in real time and screen-sharing exactly what they built and how.** (Kat Getzy) Cat Labs exists for this: at its 'How I Built This' events people show the result for one minute and then four minutes of the actual prompts and tools, because trust in AI claims is at an all-time low and grifters should be told 'share your screen, prove it'. _[Cat Labs, Claude Code]_ — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **These models are the dumbest they will ever be; even with zero further improvement we would spend 20 years deploying them across the economy, so becoming literate now is one of the most important things you can do.** (Kat Getzy) Kat says intelligence is increasing exponentially and the world in a few years will look wildly different. — [2026-08-17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BS2FoFnt-E0)
- **Use emojis only where they make sense for the content that follows; random emojis are a tell of AI-generated text.** An arrow for archery and a basketball for basketball reads as human; AI tends to throw in unrelated emojis. — [2026-08-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qCAHgny6a4Q)
- **Write your own About rather than using LinkedIn's built-in AI writer; what you write yourself is currently better, and AI is only a Hail Mary when you have no time.** The hosts say listeners will do a better job after the episode's frameworks. — [2026-08-24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qCAHgny6a4Q)
- **Remove the third-person opening paragraph that reads like LinkedIn's AI generator; it merely summarizes experience visible elsewhere and weakens a strong first-person profile.** Cherie recognized the standard AI-generated tone immediately and says the profile is stronger without it; stick to first person throughout. — [2026-08-31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nu45PVvbkxA)
- **In the age of AI everybody's default writing has improved, so you must be even better than that baseline to stand out as a communicator.** (Glenn Kramon) Kramon encourages students to use AI, but former students now managing teams call him complaining about 'AI slop' because everyone's emails sound the same; the hosts add that AI trains on existing writing and drags everyone to the same baseline. _[ChatGPT]_ — [2026-09-07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6SIi_BQ0p2g)

### Guest life story & lessons (8)

- **Holly is motivated by the intrinsic value of work and helping others succeed, with money as a measuring stick rather than the goal; she wouldn't move if she won the lottery.** (Holly MacDonald) She values high-quality time with family over quantity and is fine with work travel as long as time at home is high value. She recommends Arthur Brooks' 'From Strength to Strength' for thinking about arcs and phases of life, and says priorities become clearer over time so don't be too hard on yourself early on. _[From Strength to Strength (Arthur Brooks)]_ — [2026-01-19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N3dGhIFWMNY)
- **When life collapses you can choose to be the phoenix and treat it as happening for you, not to you, finding meaning through ACT rather than 'everything happens for a reason'.** (Sabrina Zohar) Sabrina owned a clothing company and was on the Shark Tank set about to pitch Mark Cuban when she was sent home, then again a month later, then her dog died a month after that; she rebuilt as a dating coach via TikTok. Growth shows in small moments like not freaking out when a guy cuts you off. _[Shark Tank, ACT (Acceptance and Commitment Therapy), TikTok]_ — [2026-02-23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=42BkJGOjQLo)
- **Founders podcast is worth listening to for the connections David Senra draws across deep analyses of historical and modern founders.** (Evan Spiegel) Asked what he listens to, Spiegel names Founders; his dream podcast guest would be anyone who can speak to America's long-term success. Snap's one-sentence plan for the next five years is making computing more human. _[Founders podcast, David Senra]_ — [2026-05-25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0IYBJXS-1t8)
- **Careers that look glamorous from outside are often driven by rock-bottom moments: after Glee, none of the cast could get hired because nobody could see them as anything but their characters.** (Jenna Ushkowitz) Jenna pivoted to producing and back to Broadway, which was terrifying because everyone was watching what she would do next; Ally says the biggest parts of her journey came from rock bottom, including becoming a multi-hyphenate out of need. _[Glee, Broadway]_ — [2026-06-01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAt9bx4ENpA)
- **Feeling powerless as a child became the fuel to help the most powerless people in the world, but that same drive had a shadow side that pushed her to unhealthy work levels.** (Jane Marie Chen) Jane's team invented a low-cost incubator for premature babies at Stanford in 2008; she moved to India, set a goal of saving one million babies, and 'gave her soul' to it for ten years until a setback forced the company to shut down and she felt like a complete failure. Her line: 'my pain became my purpose.' _[Embrace, Stanford]_ — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **Reaching the goal (one million babies saved) matters less than learning that it does not define all of who you are.** (Jane Marie Chen) Jane stepped down from Embrace in May after the million-baby goal was reached this year; she says what she is proudest of is learning to 'embrace myself', and now teaches leadership coaching at corporate and individual levels via janemariechen.com. _[Embrace]_ — [2026-07-06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jGj7bm3v0kY)
- **Education is freedom: material things can be taken from you, but knowledge cannot, and it buys freedom of time, choice and exploring the world.** (Jim Keyes) Keyes's father never made it past sixth grade and was tied to his factory machine; when young Jim offered to work to put plumbing in the house, his father said no, learning was his number one job. The hosts' catchphrase 'information is power' is a variant: without information you cannot make informed decisions, and they see education as the tool for socioeconomic mobility in America. _[Education Is Freedom]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
- **China's opening was a once-in-history business moment: the Beijing government flew to Dallas to ask 7-Eleven for 500 stores in time for the 2008 Olympics and delivered.** (Jim Keyes) 7-Eleven already had stores in Hong Kong and Guangdong but none in mainland China; the request came a year and a half before the Olympics and seemed impossible. Keyes recalls Beijing going from more bicycles than cars to Mercedes everywhere in five years. _[7-Eleven, Beijing Olympics]_ — [2026-07-20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bq53ee_o4AM)
